<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 97A. GAME AND FISH</h2>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.97A.001"><b>97A.001</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.001">15.001</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL PROVISIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.011">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.011 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>This chapter and chapters 97B and 97C may be cited as the "game and fish laws."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.1.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.015">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.015 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The terms defined in this section apply to this chapter and chapters 97B and 97C.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Angling; angler.</span></h2>
          <p>"Angling" means taking fish with a hook and line. An "angler" is a person who takes fish by angling.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Big game.</span></h2>
          <p>"Big game" means deer, moose, elk, bear, antelope, and caribou.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Bonus permit.</span></h2>
          <p>"Bonus permit" means a license to take deer by archery or firearms, in addition to deer authorized to be taken under regular firearms or archery licenses, or a license issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.441#stat.97A.441.7">97A.441, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Bow fishing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Bow fishing" means taking native rough fish and common carp by archery where the arrows are tethered or controlled by an attached line.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Buy.</span></h2>
          <p>"Buy" includes barter, exchange for consideration, offer to buy, or attempt to buy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Camp.</span></h2>
          <p>"Camp" means the temporary abode of a person fishing, hunting, trapping, vacationing, or touring, while on a trip or tour including resorts, tourist camps, and other establishments providing temporary lodging.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Certifiable diseases.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Certifiable diseases" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.4982">17.4982</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Chub.</span></h2>
          <p>"Chub" means shortnose cisco, shortjaw cisco, longjaw cisco, bloater, kiyi, blackfin cisco, and deepwater cisco.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Cisco.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cisco" means <i>Coregonus artedii</i> and includes lake herring and tullibee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Closed season.</span></h2>
          <p>"Closed season" means the period when a specified protected wild animal may not be taken.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Commercial fishing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commercial fishing" means taking fish, except minnows, for sale.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Condemnation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Condemnation" means the exercise of the power of eminent domain in the manner provided under chapter 117.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Contraband.</span></h2>
          <p>"Contraband" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a wild animal taken, bought, sold, transported, or possessed in violation of the game and fish laws, and all instrumentalities and devices used in taking wild animals in violation of the game and fish laws that are subject to confiscation; and</p>
          <p>(2) wild rice and other aquatic vegetation harvested, bought, sold, transported, or possessed in violation of chapter 84.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Conviction.</span></h2>
          <p>"Conviction" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a final conviction after a trial or a plea of guilty;</p>
          <p>(2) a forfeiture of cash or collateral deposited to guarantee an appearance of a defendant in court, if the forfeiture has not been vacated or the court has not reinstated the trial within 15 days after the forfeiture; or</p>
          <p>(3) a breach of a condition of release without bail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Dark house.</span></h2>
          <p>"Dark house" means a structure set on the ice of state waters that is darkened to view fish in the water beneath the structure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.14a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14a.<span class="headnote">Deer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Deer" means white-tailed or mule deer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Designated trout lake; designated trout stream.</span></h2>
          <p>"Designated trout lake" or "designated trout stream" means a lake or stream designated by the commissioner as a trout lake or a trout stream under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.005">97C.005</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Director.</span></h2>
          <p>"Director" means the director of any or all of the Divisions of Enforcement, Fisheries, Wildlife, and Ecological Services unless a specific division is identified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Division.</span></h2>
          <p>"Division" means any or all of the Divisions of Enforcement, Fisheries, Wildlife, and Ecological Services unless a specific division is identified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Enforcement officer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Enforcement officer" means the commissioner, the director of the Enforcement Division, or a conservation officer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Firearm.</span></h2>
          <p>"Firearm" means a gun that discharges shot or a projectile by means of an explosive, a gas, or compressed air.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Firearms safety certificate.</span></h2>
          <p>"Firearms safety certificate" means the certificate issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.015">97B.015</a> or an equivalent certificate issued by another state or other evidence that meets with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.020">97B.020</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Fish house.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fish house" means a structure set on the ice of state waters to provide shelter while taking fish by angling.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Fur-bearing animals.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fur-bearing animals" means mammals that are protected wild animals, except big game.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Game.</span></h2>
          <p>"Game" means big game and small game.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Game birds.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Game birds" means migratory waterfowl, ring-necked pheasant, ruffed grouse, sharp-tailed grouse, Canada spruce grouse, prairie chickens, gray partridge, bobwhite quail, wild turkeys, coots, gallinules, sora and Virginia rails, mourning dove, sandhill crane, American woodcock, and common snipe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Game fish.</span></h2>
          <p>"Game fish" means fish from the following families and species: Acipenseridae (lake sturgeon and shovelnose sturgeon), Anguillidae (American eel), Centrarchidae (black crappie; largemouth bass; rock bass; smallmouth bass; white crappie; and sunfishes, including bluegill, green sunfish, longear sunfish, orangespotted sunfish, pumpkinseed, and warmouth), Esocidae (muskellunge and northern pike), Gadidae (burbot), Ictaluridae (blue catfish, channel catfish, and flathead catfish), Moronidae (white bass and yellow bass), Percidae (sauger, walleye, and yellow perch), Polyodontidae (paddlefish), and Salmonidae (Atlantic salmon, brook trout, brown trout, chinook salmon, cisco (tullibee), coho salmon, kokanee salmon, lake trout, lake whitefish, pink salmon, and rainbow trout). Game fish includes hybrids of game fish.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.25a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25a.<span class="headnote">Guardian.</span></h2>
          <p>"Guardian" means a legal guardian of a person under age 16, or a person 18 or older who has been authorized by the parent or legal guardian to supervise the person under age 16.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Hunting.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hunting" means taking birds or mammals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.26a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26a.<span class="headnote">In-the-round.</span></h2>
          <p>"In-the-round" means fish with heads, tails, fins, skins, and scales intact.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.26b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26b.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.26c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26c.<span class="headnote">Immediately released or immediately returned to the water.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Immediately released" or "immediately returned to the water" means that a fish must not be retained longer than is needed at the site of capture to unhook, identify, measure, or photograph the fish. Placing a fish on a stringer, in a live well, or in a cooler, bucket, or other container is not "immediately released" or "immediately returned to the water."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">License.</span></h2>
          <p>"License" means a license or stamp issued under the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.27a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27a.<span class="headnote">License identification number.</span></h2>
          <p>"License identification number" means a verification number issued under the authority of the commissioner in conjunction with the electronic purchase of a license or stamp and valid until the license is received by the purchaser.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.27b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27b.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.27c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27c.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Migratory waterfowl.</span></h2>
          <p>"Migratory waterfowl" means brant, ducks, geese, tundra swans, trumpeter swans, and whooper swans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Minnows.</span></h2>
          <p>"Minnows" means:</p>
          <p>(1) members of the minnow family, Cyprinidae, except carp and goldfish;</p>
          <p>(2) members of the mudminnow family, Umbridae;</p>
          <p>(3) members of the sucker family, Catostomidae, not over 12 inches in length;</p>
          <p>(4) bullheads, ciscoes, lake whitefish, goldeyes, and mooneyes, not over seven inches long;</p>
          <p>(5) leeches; and</p>
          <p>(6) tadpole madtoms (willow cats) and stonecats.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Minnow dealer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Minnow dealer" means a person taking minnows for sale, buying minnows for resale, selling minnows at wholesale, or transporting minnows for sale.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.31">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31.<span class="headnote">Minnow retailer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Minnow retailer" means a person selling minnows at retail from an established place of business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.32">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32.<span class="headnote">Motor vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p>"Motor vehicle" means a self-propelled vehicle or a vehicle propelled or drawn by a self-propelled vehicle that is operated on a highway, on a railroad track, on the ground, in the water, or in the air.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.32a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32a.<span class="headnote">Muzzleloader season.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Muzzleloader season" means the deer season open only for legal muzzle-loading firearms, as prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.32b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32b.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 32d]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.32c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32c.<span class="headnote">Native rough fish.</span></h2>
          <p>"Native rough fish" means Amiidae (bowfin), Catostomidae (bigmouth, smallmouth, and black buffalo; white, blue, spotted, and longnose sucker; northern hogsucker; quillback; river and highfin carpsucker; and black, river, shorthead, golden, silver, and greater redhorse), Hiodontidae (goldeye and mooneye), Ictaluridae (black, brown, and yellow bullhead), Lepisosteidae (longnose and shortnose gar), and Sciaenidae (freshwater drum), except for any fish species listed as endangered, threatened, or of special concern in Minnesota Rules, chapter 6134.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.32d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32d.<span class="headnote">Native swan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Native swan" means a trumpeter swan or a tundra swan but does not include a mute swan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.33">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33.<span class="headnote">Nonresident.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nonresident" means a person who is not a resident.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.34">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 34.<span class="headnote">Open season.</span></h2>
          <p>"Open season" means the period when a specified protected wild animal may be taken.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.35">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 35.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means an individual only if used in reference to issuing licenses to take wild animals, but otherwise means an individual, firm, partnership, joint stock company, association, or public or private corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.35a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 35a.<span class="headnote">Portable shelter.</span></h2>
          <p>"Portable shelter" means a fish house, dark house, or other shelter that is set on the ice of state waters to provide shelter and that collapses, folds, or is disassembled for transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.36">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36.<span class="headnote">Possession.</span></h2>
          <p>"Possession" means both actual and constructive possession and control of the things referred to.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.37">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37.<span class="headnote">Predator.</span></h2>
          <p>"Predator" means a wolf, coyote, fox, lynx, or bobcat.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.37a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37a.<span class="headnote">Processing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Processing" means rendering a species of aquatic life for food, bait, or other purposes so that it is no longer alive.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.38">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 38.<span class="headnote">Protected birds.</span></h2>
          <p>"Protected birds" means all birds except unprotected birds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.39">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 39.<span class="headnote">Protected wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>"Protected wild animals" means big game, small game, game fish, native rough fish, minnows, leeches, chubs, frogs, turtles, clams, mussels, wolf, mourning doves, bats, snakes, salamanders, lizards, any animal species listed as endangered, threatened, or of special concern in Minnesota Rules, chapter 6134, and wild animals that are protected by a restriction in the time or manner of taking, other than a restriction in the use of artificial lights, poison, or motor vehicles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.40">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 40.<span class="headnote">Public access.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public access" means an access that is publicly owned and accessible to the public without charge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.41">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 41.<span class="headnote">Public waters.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public waters" means waters defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.15">103G.005, subdivision 15</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.41a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 41a.<span class="headnote">Regular firearms season.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Regular firearms season" means any of the firearms deer seasons prescribed by the commissioner that begin in November, exclusive of the muzzleloader season.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.42">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 42.<span class="headnote">Resident.</span></h2>
          <p>"Resident" means:</p>
          <p>(1) an individual who is a citizen of the United States or a resident alien, and has maintained a legal residence in the state at least the immediately preceding 60 days;</p>
          <p>(2) a nonresident under the age of 21 who is the child of a resident;</p>
          <p>(3) a domestic corporation; or</p>
          <p>(4) a foreign corporation authorized to do business in the state that has conducted a licensed business at a location within the state for at least ten years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.42a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 42a.<span class="headnote">Restitution value of the wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>"Restitution value of the wild animals" means the total value of the wild animals taken in a violation based on:</p>
          <p>(1) the values established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.345">97A.345</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) the values determined by the court under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.341#stat.97A.341.4">97A.341, subdivision 4</a>, if the values are not established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.345">97A.345</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.43">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 43.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 32c]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.44">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 44.<span class="headnote">Sale.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sale" means an exchange for consideration, and includes barter, offer to sell, and possession with intent to sell.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.44a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 44a.<span class="headnote">Shelter.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Shelter" means any structure, other than a self-propelled motor vehicle, that is set on the ice of state waters to provide shelter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.45">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 45.<span class="headnote">Small game.</span></h2>
          <p>"Small game" means game birds, gray squirrel, fox squirrel, cottontail rabbit, snowshoe hare, jack rabbit, raccoon, lynx, bobcat, short-tailed weasel, long-tailed weasel, wolf, red fox and gray fox, fisher, pine marten, opossum, badger, cougar, wolverine, muskrat, mink, otter, and beaver.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.46">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 46.<span class="headnote">Sunfish.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sunfish" means bluegill, pumpkinseed, green sunfish, orange spotted sunfish, longear sunfish, and warmouth. "Sunfish" includes hybrids of sunfish.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.47">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 47.<span class="headnote">Taking.</span></h2>
          <p>"Taking" means pursuing, shooting, killing, capturing, trapping, snaring, angling, spearing, or netting wild animals, or placing, setting, drawing, or using a net, trap, or other device to take wild animals. Taking includes attempting to take wild animals, and assisting another person in taking wild animals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.47a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 47a.<span class="headnote">Taxidermist.</span></h2>
          <p>"Taxidermist" means a person who engages in the business or operation of preserving or mounting wild animals or parts thereof that do not belong to the person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.48">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 48.<span class="headnote">Transport, transportation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transport, transportation" means causing or attempting to cause wild animals to be carried or moved by a device and includes accepting or receiving wild animals for transportation or shipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.49">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 49.<span class="headnote">Undressed bird.</span></h2>
          <p>"Undressed bird" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a bird, including ducks, with a fully feathered wing intact; or</p>
          <p>(2) a pheasant, Hungarian partridge, or wild turkey with one leg and foot intact.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.50">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 50.<span class="headnote">Undressed fish.</span></h2>
          <p>"Undressed fish" means fish with heads, tails, fins and skins intact, whether entrails, gills, or scales are removed or not.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.51">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 51.<span class="headnote">Unloaded.</span></h2>
          <p>"Unloaded" means, with reference to a firearm, without ammunition in the barrels and magazine, if the magazine is in the firearm. A muzzle-loading firearm is unloaded if:</p>
          <p>(1) for a flintlock ignition, it does not have priming powder in a pan;</p>
          <p>(2) for a percussion ignition, it does not have a percussion cap on a nipple;</p>
          <p>(3) for an electronic ignition system, the battery is removed and is disconnected from the firearm; and</p>
          <p>(4) for an encapsulated powder charge ignition system, the primer and powder charge are removed from the firearm.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.52">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 52.<span class="headnote">Unprotected birds.</span></h2>
          <p>"Unprotected birds" means English sparrow, starling, cormorant, common pigeon, Eurasian collared dove, chukar partridge, quail other than bobwhite quail, and mute swan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.53">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 53.<span class="headnote">Unprotected wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>"Unprotected wild animals" means wild animals that are not protected wild animals including coyote, plains pocket gopher, porcupine, striped skunk, and unprotected birds, except any animal species listed as endangered, threatened, or of special concern in Minnesota Rules, chapter 6134.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.53a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 53a.<span class="headnote">Validation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Validation" means the documentation process for taking a specific species under a license for that species, which can be completed electronically or on the corresponding paper license, permit, or endorsement to include information specified by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.54">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 54.<span class="headnote">Waters of this state; state waters.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waters of this state" and "state waters" include all boundary and inland waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.015.55">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 55.<span class="headnote">Wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>"Wild animals" means all creatures, whether dead or alive, not human, wild by nature, endowed with sensation and power of voluntary motion, and includes mammals, birds, fish, amphibians, reptiles, crustaceans, and mollusks.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.015.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.2.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.2.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 2</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/588/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 588 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.21.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.25.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/462/#laws.0.4.0">1992 c 462 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/269/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 269 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/623/#laws.1.20.0">1994 c 623 art 1 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.15.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 15</a>-17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.22.0">1996 c 410 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.77.0">1997 c 216 s 77</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/146/#laws.0.6.0">2005 c 146 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/214/#laws.0.20.0">2006 c 214 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.20.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 20</a>; art 2 s 10-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.19.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/307/#laws.0.11.0">2008 c 307 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.8.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 8</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.10.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.15.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 15</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.19.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 19</a>,20,90;       <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 3a by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 19, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivision 27a is repealed by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 51, effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52. </p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivision 53a, as added by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 20, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.021">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.021 CONSTRUCTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.021.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Code of Criminal Procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>A provision of the game and fish laws that is inconsistent with the Code of Criminal Procedure or of penal law is only effective under the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.021.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authority of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>A provision of the game and fish laws is subject to, and does not change or modify the authority of the commissioner to delegate powers, duties, and functions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.083">84.083</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.021.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Parts of wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>A provision relating to a wild animal applies in the same manner to a part of the wild animal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.021.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Dates and open seasons.</span></h2>
          <p>The dates specified in the game and fish laws and time periods prescribed for certain activities or as open season are inclusive, unless otherwise specified.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.021.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.3.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.17.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.025">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.025 OWNERSHIP OF WILD ANIMALS.</h1>
        <p>The ownership of wild animals of the state is in the state, in its sovereign capacity for the benefit of all the people of the state. A person may not acquire a property right in wild animals, or destroy them, unless authorized under the game and fish laws or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.47">17.47</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.498">17.498</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.025.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.4.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/309/#laws.0.13.0">1991 c 309 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.58.0">1996 c 410 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 290 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.028">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.028 CROP PROTECTION ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.028.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Agricultural crops" means annually seeded crops, legumes, fruit orchards, tree farms and nurseries, turf farms, and apiaries.</p>
          <p>(c) "Parcel" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.03#stat.272.03.6">272.03, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Specialty crops" means fruit orchards, vegetables, tree farms and nurseries, turf farms, and apiaries.</p>
          <p>(e) "Stored forage crops" means hay, silage, grain, or other crops that have been harvested and placed in storage for commercial livestock feeding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.028.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a statewide program to provide technical assistance to persons for the protection of agricultural crops from destruction by wild animals. As part of the program, the commissioner shall develop and identify the latest and most effective abatement techniques; acquire appropriate demonstration supplies and materials required to meet specialized needs; train property owners, field staff, public land managers, extension agents, pest control operators, and others; provide technical manuals and brochures; and provide field personnel with supplies and materials for damage abatement demonstrations and short-term assistance and for the establishment of food or lure crops where appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.028.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Emergency deterrent materials assistance.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purposes of this subdivision, "cooperative damage management agreement" means an agreement between a landowner or tenant and the commissioner that establishes a program for addressing the problem of destruction of the landowner's or tenant's specialty crops or stored forage crops by wild animals, destruction of agricultural crops by flightless Canada geese, or destruction of agricultural crops or pasture by elk within the native elk range, as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A landowner or tenant may apply to the commissioner for emergency deterrent materials assistance in controlling destruction of the landowner's or tenant's specialty crops or stored forage crops by wild animals, destruction of agricultural crops by flightless Canada geese, or destruction of agricultural crops or pasture by elk within the native elk range, as determined by the commissioner. Subject to the availability of money appropriated for this purpose, the commissioner shall provide suitable deterrent materials when the commissioner determines that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) immediate action is necessary to prevent significant damage from continuing; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a cooperative damage management agreement cannot be implemented immediately.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A person may receive emergency deterrent materials assistance under this subdivision more than once, but the cumulative total value of deterrent materials provided to a person, or for use on a parcel, may not exceed $5,000 for specialty crops, $1,500 for protecting stored forage crops other than silage or grain, $3,000 for stored silage or grain, or $1,000 for agricultural crops damaged by flightless Canada geese. The value of deterrent materials provided to a person to help protect stored forage crops, agricultural crops, or pasture from damage by elk may not exceed $5,000. If a person is a co-owner or cotenant with respect to the crops for which the deterrent materials are provided, the deterrent materials are deemed to be "provided" to the person for the purposes of this paragraph.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) As a condition of receiving emergency deterrent materials assistance under this subdivision, a landowner or tenant shall enter into a cooperative damage management agreement with the commissioner. Deterrent materials provided by the commissioner may include repellents, fencing materials, or other materials recommended in the agreement to alleviate the damage problem. If requested by a landowner or tenant, any fencing materials provided must be capable of providing long-term protection of specialty crops. A landowner or tenant who receives emergency deterrent materials assistance under this subdivision shall comply with the terms of the cooperative damage management agreement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.028.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.50.0">1993 c 172 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.2.23.0">1994 c 632 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/407/#laws.0.40.0">1996 c 407 s 40</a>,41; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.78.0">1997 c 216 s 78</a>,79; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.9.9.0">2006 c 282 art 9 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/297/#laws.1.23.0">2008 c 297 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.20.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.031">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.031 WANTON WASTE.</h1>
        <p>(a) Unless expressly allowed, a person may not wantonly waste or destroy a usable part of a protected wild animal.</p>
        <p>(b) This section does not apply to common carp.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.031.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.5.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.40.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.035">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.035 REMOVING SIGNS PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>A person may not remove or deface a Department of Natural Resources sign, without approval of the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.035.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.6.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.037">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.037 HUNTER, TRAPPER, AND ANGLER HARASSMENT PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.037.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Interference with taking wild animals prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who has the intent to prevent or disrupt another person from taking or preparing to take a wild animal or enjoyment of the out-of-doors must not disturb or interfere with that person if that person is lawfully taking or preparing to take a wild animal. "Preparing to take a wild animal" includes travel, camping, and other acts that occur on land or water where the affected person has the right or privilege to take lawfully a wild animal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.037.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disturbing wild animals prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who has the intent to prevent or disrupt a person from lawfully taking the animals may not disturb or engage in an activity that will tend to disturb wild animals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.037.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prohibition on public lands or without landowner permission.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who has intent to violate subdivision 1 or 2 may not enter or remain on public lands, or on private lands without permission of the owner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.037.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Peace officer order; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person must obey the order of a peace officer to stop the harassing conduct that violates this section if the officer observes the conduct. For purposes of this subdivision, "harassing conduct" does not include a landowner's or lessee's action to enforce the trespass law. Violation of this subdivision is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.037.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/287/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 287 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/401/#laws.0.33.0">1998 c 401 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.041">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.041 EXHIBITION OF WILDLIFE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "wildlife" means any wild mammal, wild bird, reptile, or amphibian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Possession.</span></h2>
          <p>A person connected with a commercial enterprise may not possess wildlife in captivity for public exhibition purposes, except under permit as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permit.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a permit to possess wildlife for public exhibition to an applicant qualified by education or experience in the care and treatment of wildlife. The permit fee is $10. The commissioner may prescribe terms and conditions of the permit. A permit issued under this section shall include a condition that allows an enforcement officer to enter and inspect the facilities where the wildlife covered by the permit are held in captivity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Permit application.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a permit must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a statement regarding the education or experience in the care and treatment of wildlife of the applicant and each individual employed by the applicant for that purpose;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the facilities used to keep the wildlife in captivity;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement of the number of species or subspecies of wildlife to be covered by the permit and a statement describing where and from whom the wildlife was acquired;</p>
          <p>(4) a signed agreement that the standards prescribed by the commissioner will be followed; and</p>
          <p>(5) other information requested by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Care and treatment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt, under chapter 14, reasonable standards for the care and treatment of captive wildlife for public display purposes, including standards of sanitation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Violating possession standards.</span></h2>
          <p>If a violation is found during an inspection, the commissioner shall give the permittee notice to abate the violation within an adequate time determined by the commissioner. If the violation has not been abated when the time expires, the commissioner may request the attorney general to bring an action to abate the violation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.041.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Exemption for zoos, circuses, pet shops.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to a publicly owned zoo or wildlife exhibit, privately owned traveling zoo or circus, or a pet shop.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.041.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.7.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.043">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.043 FISH CONSUMPTION ADVISORIES.</h1>
        <p class="in">The commissioner of natural resources, in cooperation with the commissioner of health, shall ensure that fish consumption advisories are displayed in at least four different languages, one of which must be English, to fairly represent the population of the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.043.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.67.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 67</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.045">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.045 COMMISSIONER; GENERAL POWERS AND DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties; generally.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall do all things the commissioner determines are necessary to preserve, protect, and propagate desirable species of wild animals. The commissioner shall make special provisions for the management of fish and wildlife to ensure recreational opportunities for anglers and hunters. The commissioner shall acquire wild animals for breeding or stocking and may dispose of or destroy undesirable or predatory wild animals and their dens, nests, houses, or dams.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding chapters 17 and 35, the commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of agriculture and the executive director of the Board of Animal Health, may capture or control nonnative or domestic animals that are released, have escaped, or are otherwise running at large and causing damage to natural resources or agricultural lands, or that are posing a threat to wildlife, domestic animals, or human health. The commissioner may work with other agencies to assist in the capture or control and may authorize persons to take such animals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Power to protect wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may protect a species of wild animal in addition to the protection provided by the game and fish laws, by further limiting or closing seasons or areas of the state, or by reducing limits in areas of the state, if the commissioner determines the action is necessary to prevent unnecessary depletion or extinction, or to promote the propagation and reproduction of the animal.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may protect a species of wild animal in the state by emergency rule adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.13">84.027, subdivision 13</a>, by prohibiting or allowing taking of the animal whether or not the animal is protected under the game and fish laws. The commissioner must make findings of the necessity of a rule authorized under this paragraph and may authorize taking by special permit with or without fee under conditions prescribed in the rule by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may protect a species of wild animal in the state by emergency rule adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.13">84.027, subdivision 13</a>, by allowing importation, transportation, or possession of the wild animal or prohibiting these activities except by special permit with or without fee under conditions prescribed in the rule by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Power to modify dates of seasons.</span></h2>
          <p>If the statutory opening date of a season for taking protected wild animals, except a season prescribed under federal regulations, is not on a Saturday, the commissioner may designate the nearest Saturday to the statutory date as the opening day of the season. If the statutory closing date falls on a Saturday, the commissioner may extend it through the following day.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Boundary waters.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may regulate the taking, possession, and transportation of wild animals from state and international boundary waters. The rules may include:</p>
          <p>(1) special seasons for taking fish; and</p>
          <p>(2) restrictions on the limits of fish that may be taken, possessed, or transported from international boundary waters by a person possessing both a Minnesota angling license and an angling license from an adjacent Canadian province.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Power to prescribe form of permits and licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), the commissioner may prescribe the form of permits, licenses, and tags issued under the game and fish laws.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must offer an applicant for an angling, trapping, or hunting license, including a special permit issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.401">97A.401</a>, the option of receiving the license in either a paper or paperless format and must provide an applicant with a paperless license unless the applicant requests a paper license. This paragraph applies to both annual and lifetime licenses. The commissioner must ensure that a person authorized to issue an annual license described in this paragraph has the ability to issue paperless licenses.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Duty to disseminate information.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall collect, compile, publish, and disseminate statistics, bulletins, and information related to conservation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Duty to encourage stamp design and purchases.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall encourage the purchase of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) Minnesota migratory-waterfowl stamps by nonhunters interested in migratory waterfowl preservation and habitat development;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) pheasant stamps by persons interested in pheasant habitat improvement;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) trout-and-salmon stamps by persons interested in trout and salmon stream and lake improvement;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) turkey stamps by persons interested in stamp collecting; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) walleye stamps by persons interested in walleye stocking and stamp collecting.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner shall make rules governing contests for selecting a design for each stamp, including those stamps not required to be in possession while taking game or fish. The commissioner shall ensure that stamp design and characteristics are consistent with the design and characteristics that are sought by pictorial-stamp collectors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.91.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 91</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Notice of rulemaking.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to notice requirements under chapter 14, the commissioner shall attempt to notify persons or groups of persons affected by rules adopted under the game and fish laws by public announcements, press releases, and other appropriate means as determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Reciprocal agreements on violations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may enter into reciprocal agreements with game and fish authorities in other states and the United States government to provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) revocation of the appropriate Minnesota game and fish licenses of Minnesota residents for violations of game and fish laws committed in signatory jurisdictions that result in license revocation in that jurisdiction;</p>
          <p>(2) reporting convictions and license revocations of residents of signatory states for violations of game and fish laws of Minnesota to game and fish authorities in the nonresident's state of residence; and</p>
          <p>(3) release upon signature without posting of bail for residents of signatory states accused of game and fish law violations in this state, providing for recovery, in the resident jurisdiction, of fines levied if the citation is not answered in this state.</p>
          <p>(b) As used in this subdivision, "conviction" includes a plea of guilty or a forfeiture of bail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Power to prevent or control wildlife disease.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If the commissioner determines that action is necessary to prevent or control a wildlife disease, the commissioner may prevent or control wildlife disease in a species of wild animal in addition to the protection provided by the game and fish laws by further limiting, closing, expanding, or opening seasons or areas of the state; by reducing or increasing limits in areas of the state; by establishing disease management zones; by authorizing free licenses; by allowing shooting from motor vehicles by persons designated by the commissioner; by issuing replacement licenses for sick animals; by requiring sample collection from hunter-harvested animals; by limiting wild animal possession, transportation, and disposition; and by restricting wildlife feeding.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner shall restrict wildlife feeding within the modified accredited bovine tuberculosis zone proposed by the Board of Animal Health. In addition to any other penalties provided by law, a person who violates wildlife feeding restrictions required under this paragraph may not obtain a hunting license to take a wild animal for two years after the date of conviction.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner may prevent or control wildlife disease in a species of wild animal in the state by posting restrictions on public access to active disease areas or by emergency rule adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.13">84.027, subdivision 13</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Establishing fees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the commissioner may, by written order published in the State Register, establish fees providing for the use of state wildlife management area or aquatic management area lands for specific purposes, including dog trials, special events, and commercial uses. The fees are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.045.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.91.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 91</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.045.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.8.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/19/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 19 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.11.0">1991 c 259 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 231 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/269/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 269 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.18.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.15.0">1997 c 226 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.104.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.49.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/146/#laws.0.8.0">2005 c 146 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.5.1.0">2006 c 281 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.79.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 79</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/274/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 274 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.11.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/94/#laws.1.89.0">2009 c 94 art 1 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.10.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.68.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.41.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 5 by Laws 2023, chapter 60, article 4, section 41, is effective March 1, 2026. Laws 2023, chapter 60, article 4, section 41, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0451">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0451 AUTHORITY FOR USING EMERGENCY RULES PROCEDURE; EXPIRATION OF AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.0451.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When to use emergency rulemaking.</span></h2>
          <p>When the commissioner is directed by statute, federal law, or court order to adopt, amend, suspend, or repeal a rule in a manner that does not allow for compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.14">14.14</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.28">14.28</a>, or if the commissioner is expressly required or authorized by statute to adopt emergency rules, the commissioner shall adopt emergency rules in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.0451">97A.0451</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.0459">97A.0459</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.0451.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">180-day time limit.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless the commissioner is directed by federal law or court order to adopt, amend, suspend, or repeal a rule in a manner that does not allow for compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.14">14.14</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.28">14.28</a>, the commissioner may not adopt an emergency rule later than 180 days after the effective date of the statutory authority, except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.13">84.027, subdivision 13</a>. If emergency rules are not adopted within the time allowed, the authority for the rules expires. The time limit of this section does not include any days used for review by the attorney general. If the 180-day period expires while the attorney general is reviewing the rule and the attorney general disapproves the rule, the commissioner may resubmit the rule to the attorney general after taking corrective action. The resubmission must occur within five working days after the commissioner receives written notice of disapproval. If the rule is again disapproved by the attorney general, it is withdrawn.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0451.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.40.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 40</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0452">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0452 NOTICE OF PROPOSED ADOPTION OF EMERGENCY RULE.</h1>
        <p>The proposed emergency rule must be published with a notice of intent to adopt emergency rules in the State Register, and the same notice must be mailed to all persons registered with the commissioner to receive notice of any rulemaking proceedings. The notice must include a statement advising the public that a free copy of the proposed rule is available on request from the commissioner and that notice of the date of submission of the proposed emergency rule to the attorney general will be mailed to any person requesting to receive the notice. For at least 25 days after publication the commissioner shall afford all interested persons an opportunity to submit data and views on the proposed emergency rule in writing. The notice must also include the date on which the 25-day comment period ends.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0452.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.41.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0453">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0453 NOTICE TO COMMITTEES FOR FEES FIXED BY RULE.</h1>
        <p>Before the commissioner submits notice to the State Register of intent to adopt emergency rules that establish or adjust fees, the commissioner shall send a copy of the notice and the proposed rules to the chairs of the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee and the senate Committee on Finance.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0453.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.42.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.3.8.0">1996 c 305 art 3 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0454">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0454 MODIFYING PROPOSED EMERGENCY RULE.</h1>
        <p>The proposed emergency rule may be modified if the modifications are supported by the data and views submitted to the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0454.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.43.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 43</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0455">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0455 SUBMITTING PROPOSED EMERGENCY RULE TO ATTORNEY GENERAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.0455.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Submission.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall submit to the attorney general the proposed emergency rule as published, with any modifications. On the same day that it is submitted, the commissioner shall mail notice of the submission to all persons who requested to be informed that the proposed emergency rule has been submitted to the attorney general. If the proposed emergency rule has been modified, the notice must state that fact, and must state that a free copy of the proposed emergency rule, as modified, is available upon request from the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.0455.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The attorney general shall review the proposed emergency rule as to its legality, review its form to the extent the form relates to legality, and shall approve or disapprove the proposed emergency rule and any modifications on the tenth working day following the date of receipt of the proposed emergency rule from the commissioner. The attorney general shall send a statement of reasons for disapproval of the rule to the commissioner, the chief administrative law judge, the Legislative Coordinating Commission, and to the revisor of statutes.</p>
          <p>(b) The attorney general shall disregard any error or defect in the proceeding due to the commissioner's failure to satisfy any procedural requirement imposed by law or rule if the attorney general finds:</p>
          <p>(1) that the failure did not deprive any person or entity of an opportunity to participate meaningfully in the rulemaking process; or</p>
          <p>(2) that the commissioner has taken corrective action to cure the error or defect so that the failure did not deprive any person or entity of an opportunity to participate meaningfully in the rulemaking process.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.0455.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Costs.</span></h2>
          <p>The attorney general shall assess the commissioner for the actual cost of processing rules under this section. The commissioner shall include in the department's budget money to pay the attorney general's assessment. Receipts from the assessment must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0455.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.44.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/254/#laws.1.22.0">1998 c 254 art 1 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0456">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0456 EFFECTIVE DATE OF EMERGENCY RULE.</h1>
        <p>The emergency rule takes effect five working days after approval by the attorney general. The attorney general shall file two copies of the approved emergency rule with the secretary of state. The secretary of state shall forward one copy of each approved and filed emergency rule to the revisor of statutes. Failure of the attorney general to approve or disapprove a proposed emergency rule within ten working days is approval.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0456.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.45.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 45</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0457">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0457 PUBLISHING APPROVAL.</h1>
        <p>As soon as practicable, notice of the attorney general's decision must be published in the State Register and the adopted rule must be published in the manner as provided for adopted rules in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.18">14.18</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0457.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.46.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 46</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0458">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0458 EFFECTIVE PERIOD OF EMERGENCY RULE.</h1>
        <p>Emergency rules adopted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.0451">97A.0451</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.0459">97A.0459</a> shall be effective for the period stated in the notice of intent to adopt emergency rules which may not be longer than 180 days. The emergency rules may be continued in effect for an additional period of up to 180 days if the commissioner gives notice of continuation by publishing notice in the State Register and mailing the same notice to all persons registered with the commissioner to receive notice of any rulemaking proceedings. The continuation is not effective until these notices have been mailed. No emergency rule may remain in effect on a date 361 days after its original effective date. The emergency rules may not be continued in effect after 360 days without following the procedure of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.14">14.14</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.28">14.28</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0458.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.47.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 47</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.0459">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.0459 APPROVING FORM OF EMERGENCY RULE.</h1>
        <p>No approved emergency rule shall be filed with the secretary of state or published in the State Register unless the revisor of statutes has certified that the emergency rule's form is approved.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.0459.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.48.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 48</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.051">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.051 PUBLISHING LAW SUMMARY; PENALTIES FOR RULE VIOLATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.051.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.20.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.051.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Summary of fish and game laws.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall prepare a summary of the hunting and fishing laws and rules and deliver a sufficient supply to license vendors.</p>
          <p>(b) At the beginning of the summary, under the heading "Trespass," the commissioner shall summarize the trespass provisions under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.001">97B.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.945">97B.945</a>, state that conservation officers and peace officers must enforce the trespass laws, and state the penalties for trespassing.</p>
          <p>(c) In the summary, the commissioner shall, under the heading "Duty to Render Aid," summarize the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.662">609.662</a> and state the penalties for failure to render aid to a person injured by gunshot.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.051.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/155/#laws.0.5.0">1989 c 155 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.051.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rules have force and effect of law.</span></h2>
          <p>When a rule is effective, it has the force and effect of law. Violation of a rule has the same penalty as a violation of the law under which the rule was adopted.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.051.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.9.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/155/#laws.0.3.0">1989 c 155 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/243/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 243 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.12.0">1991 c 259 s 12</a>-14,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 585 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.11.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.46.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.052">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.052 PEACE OFFICER TRAINING ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.052.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established; sources.</span></h2>
          <p>The peace officer training account is created in the game and fish fund in the state treasury. Revenue from the portion of the surcharges assessed to criminal and traffic offenders in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/357.021#stat.357.021.7">357.021, subdivision 7</a>, paragraph (a), clause (1), shall be deposited in the account. Money in the account may be spent only for the purposes provided in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.052.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purposes of account.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the peace officer training account may only be spent by the commissioner for peace officer training for employees of the Department of Natural Resources who are licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.84">626.84</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.863">626.863 </a>to enforce game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.052.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.9.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.13.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.055">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.055 GAME AND FISH FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>The game and fish fund is established as a fund in the state treasury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Receipts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall credit to the game and fish fund all money received under the game and fish laws and all income from state lands acquired by purchase or gift for game or fish purposes, including receipts from:</p>
          <p>(1) licenses and permits issued;</p>
          <p>(2) fines and forfeited bail;</p>
          <p>(3) sales of contraband, wild animals, and other property under the control of the division, except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.225#stat.97A.225.8">97A.225, subdivision 8</a>, clause (2);</p>
          <p>(4) fees from advanced education courses for hunters and trappers;</p>
          <p>(5) reimbursements of expenditures by the division;</p>
          <p>(6) contributions to the division; and</p>
          <p>(7) revenue credited to the game and fish fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.94">297A.94, paragraph </a>(h), clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Federal aid disposition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Federal aid reimbursements under the Federal Aid in Wildlife Restoration Act, United States Code, title 16, sections 669 to 669i, shall be deposited in the game and fish fund.</p>
