          <p>(b) Federal aid reimbursements under the Federal Aid in Fish Restoration Act, United States Code, title 16, sections 777 to 777k, shall be deposited in the game and fish fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Certified costs.</span></h2>
          <p>Money for the certified costs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.0385">89.0385 </a>is transferred annually for reimbursement of certified costs on state lands acquired by purchase or gift for game and fish purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Game and fish fund fees.</span></h2>
          <p>To reduce yearly fluctuations of the game and fish fund balance and to provide improved long-range planning of the fund, the policy of the state is to make fee adjustments as part of the budget process. Agency responsibilities are:</p>
          <p>(1) The commissioner of natural resources must make specific requests for fee adjustments for all receipt items in the game and fish fund as a part of the fee report.</p>
          <p>(2) The commissioner of management and budget must review the fee report and make recommendations for each fee. The commissioner of management and budget must submit a six-year projection on revenues and expenditures to the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Game and fish annual reports.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) By December 15 each year, the commissioner shall submit to the legislative committees having jurisdiction over appropriations and the environment and natural resources reports on each of the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the amount of revenue from the following and purposes for which expenditures were made:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) the small-game license surcharge under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.4">97A.475, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) the Minnesota migratory-waterfowl stamp under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.5">97A.475, subdivision 5</a>, clause (1);</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) the trout-and-salmon stamp under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.10">97A.475, subdivision 10</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) the pheasant stamp under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.5">97A.475, subdivision 5</a>, clause (2);</p>
          <p class="in">(v) the wild-turkey management account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.075#stat.97A.075.5">97A.075, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(vi) the deer license donations and surcharges under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475, subdivisions 3</a>, paragraph (b), and 3a; and</p>
          <p class="in">(vii) the walleye stamp under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.10a">97A.475, subdivision 10a</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the amounts available under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.075#stat.97A.075.1">97A.075, subdivision 1</a>, paragraphs (b) and (c), and the purposes for which these amounts were spent;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) money credited to the game and fish fund under this section and purposes for which expenditures were made from the fund;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) outcome goals for the expenditures from the game and fish fund; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) summary and comments of citizen oversight committee reviews under subdivision 4b.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The report must include the commissioner's recommendations, if any, for changes in the laws relating to the stamps and surcharge referenced in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.58.0">2001 c 161 s 58</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.4b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Fish and Wildlife Advisory Committee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall appoint a committee of at least 15 affected persons to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) review the reports prepared under subdivision 4;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) review annual outcomes achieved from game and fish fund expenditures; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) make recommendations to the legislature and the commissioner for desired outcomes related to:</p>
          <p>(i) protecting, restoring, and enhancing fish and wildlife habitat;</p>
          <p>(ii) fish and wildlife population management;</p>
          <p>(iii) fish and wildlife monitoring and research;</p>
          <p>(iv) communications and engagement; and</p>
          <p class="in">(v) improvements in the management and use of money in the game and fish fund.</p>
          <p>(b) The chair of the Fish and Wildlife Advisory Committee is appointed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) By September 15 each year, the committee must submit a report to the commissioner and to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over natural resources finance and policy. Each even-numbered year, the report must focus on biennial budget outcomes achieved from game and fish fund expenditures. Each odd-numbered year, the report must focus on outcomes related to protecting habitat, fish and wildlife population management, monitoring and research, and communications and engagement.</p>
          <p>(d) Annually, the Fish and Wildlife Advisory Committee must hold a meeting for the public to review proposed priorities for the next reporting period. The meeting must be organized to allow virtual participation.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The committee authorized under this subdivision is not an advisory council or committee governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a> and is not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>. Committee members appointed by the commissioner may request reimbursement for mileage expenses in the same manner and amount as authorized by the commissioner's plan adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.18#stat.43A.18.2">43A.18, subdivision 2</a>. Committee members must not receive daily compensation for committee activities. The Fish and Wildlife Advisory Committee expires June 30, 2033.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Pelting fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may pay a pelting fee to a person who recovers, treats, preserves, or transports the pelt of a fur-bearing animal that is accidentally killed or is lawfully taken under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.655">97B.655</a>. The commissioner may adopt rules setting pelting fees and governing their payment. The amounts necessary to pay the fees are appropriated from the game and fish fund to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.055.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Land acquisition restriction.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475</a>, subdivision 4, revenue from the sale of game and fish licenses and permits, excluding revenue from hunting and fishing stamps, shall not be used to acquire land in fee or easement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.055.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.10.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.7.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.85.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.51.0">1993 c 172 s 51</a>,52; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 231 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 561 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/364/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 364 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.44.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/463/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 463 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.3.26.0">2000 c 488 art 3 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.105.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 105</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/376/#laws.0.6.0">2002 c 376 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.21.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.27.0">2004 c 255 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.95.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.17.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.80.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.24.0">2008 c 357 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.12.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.49.0">2011 c 107 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.10.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.2.1.0">2012 c 277 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/286/#laws.8.11.0">2014 c 286 art 8 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.15.0">2014 c 290 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.69.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.69.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.21.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.47.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.4.4.0">2024 c 90 art 4 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.056">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.056 OUTDOOR HERITAGE FUND; LESSARD-SAMS OUTDOOR HERITAGE COUNCIL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Outdoor heritage fund.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An outdoor heritage fund, under article XI, section 15, of the Minnesota Constitution, is established as an account in the state treasury. All money earned by the outdoor heritage fund must be credited to the fund. At least 99 percent of the money appropriated from the fund must be expended to restore, protect, and enhance wetlands, prairies, forests, and habitat for fish, game, and wildlife. Money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund shall not be spent to acquire property by eminent domain unless the owner requests that the owner's property be acquired by eminent domain.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of appropriations from the outdoor heritage fund, "recipient" means the entity responsible for deliverables financed by the outdoor heritage fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council of 12 members is created in the legislative branch, consisting of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) two public members appointed by the senate Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) two public members appointed by the speaker of the house;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) four public members appointed by the governor;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) two members of the senate appointed by the senate Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) two members of the house of representatives appointed by the speaker of the house.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Members appointed under paragraph (a) must not be registered lobbyists. In making appointments, the governor, senate Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration, and the speaker of the house shall consider geographic balance, gender, age, ethnicity, and varying interests including hunting and fishing. The governor's appointments to the council are subject to the advice and consent of the senate.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Public members appointed under paragraph (a) shall have practical experience or expertise or demonstrated knowledge in the science, policy, or practice of restoring, protecting, and enhancing wetlands, prairies, forests, and habitat for fish, game, and wildlife.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Legislative members appointed under paragraph (a) shall include the chairs of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over environment and natural resources finance or their designee, one member from the minority party of the senate, and one member from the minority party of the house of representatives.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Public members serve four-year terms. Appointed legislative members serve at the pleasure of the appointing authority. Public and legislative members continue to serve until their successors are appointed. Public members shall be initially appointed according to the following schedule of terms:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) two public members appointed by the governor for a term ending the first Monday in January 2011;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) one public member appointed by the senate Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration for a term ending the first Monday in January 2011;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) one public member appointed by the speaker of the house for a term ending the first Monday in January 2011;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) two public members appointed by the governor for a term ending the first Monday in January 2013;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) one public member appointed by the senate Subcommittee on Committees of the Committee on Rules and Administration for a term ending the first Monday in January 2013; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) one public member appointed by the speaker of the house for a term ending the first Monday in January 2013.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Terms, compensation, and removal of public members are as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>, except that a public member may be compensated at the rate of up to $125 a day. A vacancy on the council may be filled by the appointing authority for the remainder of the unexpired term.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) Members shall elect a chair, vice-chair, secretary, and other officers as determined by the council. The chair may convene meetings as necessary to conduct the duties prescribed by this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) The Legislative Coordinating Commission may appoint nonpartisan staff and contract with consultants as necessary to support the functions of the council. The council has final approval authority for the hiring of a candidate for executive director. Up to one percent of the money appropriated from the fund may be used to pay for administrative expenses of the council and for compensation and expense reimbursement of council members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Council recommendations.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The council shall make recommendations to the legislature on appropriations of money from the outdoor heritage fund that are consistent with the constitution and state law and that will achieve the outcomes of existing natural resource plans, including, but not limited to, the Minnesota Statewide Conservation and Preservation Plan, that directly relate to the restoration, protection, and enhancement of wetlands, prairies, forests, and habitat for fish, game, and wildlife, and that prevent forest fragmentation, encourage forest consolidation, and expand restored native prairie. In making recommendations, the council shall consider a range of options that would best restore, protect, and enhance wetlands, prairies, forests, and habitat for fish, game, and wildlife. The council's recommendations shall be submitted no later than January 15 each year. The council shall present its recommendations to the senate and house of representatives committees with jurisdiction over the environment and natural resources budget by February 15 in odd-numbered years, and within the first four weeks of the legislative session in even-numbered years. The council's budget recommendations to the legislature shall be separate from the Department of Natural Resource's budget recommendations.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) To encourage and support local conservation efforts, the council shall establish a conservation partners program. Local, regional, state, or national organizations may apply for matching grants for restoration, protection, and enhancement of wetlands, prairies, forests, and habitat for fish, game, and wildlife, prevention of forest fragmentation, encouragement of forest consolidation, and expansion of restored native prairie.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The council may work with the Clean Water Council to identify projects that are consistent with both the purpose of the outdoor heritage fund and the purpose of the clean water fund.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The council may make recommendations to the Legislative-Citizen Commission on Minnesota Resources on scientific research that will assist in restoring, protecting, and enhancing wetlands, prairies, forests, and habitat for fish, game, and wildlife, preventing forest fragmentation, encouraging forest consolidation, and expanding restored native prairie.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Recommendations of the council, including approval of recommendations for the outdoor heritage fund, require an affirmative vote of at least nine members of the council.</p>
          <p>(f) The council may work with the Clean Water Council, the Legislative-Citizen Commission on Minnesota Resources, the Board of Water and Soil Resources, soil and water conservation districts, and experts from Minnesota State Colleges and Universities and the University of Minnesota in developing the council's recommendations.</p>
          <p>(g) The council shall develop and implement a process that ensures that citizens and potential recipients of funds are included throughout the process, including the development and finalization of the council's recommendations. The process must include a fair, equitable, and thorough process for reviewing requests for funding and a clear and easily understood process for ranking projects.</p>
          <p>(h) The council shall use the regions of the state based upon the ecological sections and subsections developed by the Department of Natural Resources and establish objectives for each region and subregion to achieve the purposes of the fund outlined in the state constitution.</p>
          <p>(i) The council shall develop and submit to the Legislative Coordinating Commission plans for the first ten years of funding, and a framework for 25 years of funding, consistent with statutory and constitutional requirements. The council may use existing plans from other legislative, state, and federal sources, as applicable.</p>
          <p>(j) By July 1 each year, the council shall provide counties with a list of project proposals that include potential fee title land acquisitions in the county that is based on that year's funding requests received by the council from nongovernmental organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Conflict of interest.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A council member may not be an advocate for or against a council action or vote on any action that may be a conflict of interest. A conflict of interest must be disclosed as soon as it is discovered. The council shall follow the policies and requirements related to conflicts of interest developed by the Office of Grants Management under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For the purposes of this section, a "conflict of interest" exists when a person has an organizational conflict of interest or direct financial interests and those interests present the appearance that it will be difficult for the person to impartially fulfill the person's duty. An "organizational conflict of interest" exists when a person has an affiliation with an organization that is subject to council activities, which presents the appearance of a conflict between organizational interests and council member duties. An "organizational conflict of interest" does not exist if the person's only affiliation with an organization is being a member of the organization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Open meetings.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Meetings of the council and other groups the council may establish are subject to chapter 13D. Except where prohibited by law, the council shall establish additional processes to broaden public involvement in all aspects of its deliberations, including recording meetings, video conferencing, and publishing minutes. For the purposes of this subdivision, a meeting occurs when a quorum is present and the members receive information or take action on any matter relating to the duties of the council. The quorum requirement for the council shall be seven members.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Travel to and from scheduled and publicly noticed site visits by council members for the purposes of receiving information is not a violation of paragraph (a). Any decision or agreement to make a decision during the travel is a violation of paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) For legislative members of the council, enforcement of this subdivision is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.055">3.055</a>, subdivision 2. For nonlegislative members of the council, enforcement of this subdivision is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.06">13D.06</a>, subdivisions 1 and 2.</p>
          <p>(d) Unless held at a location outside the Capitol complex, meetings of the council must be made available on a website for live video streaming and be archived on a website for playback at a later time. For meetings of the council held at a location outside the Capitol complex, the council must make meetings available via live video stream and archive the video to the extent practicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Audit.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The legislative auditor shall audit the outdoor heritage fund expenditures, including administrative and staffing expenditures, to ensure that the money is spent in compliance with all applicable law and the constitution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.187.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 187</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/91/#laws.1.11.0">2017 c 91 art 1 s 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Lands in public domain.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund shall not be used to purchase any land in fee title or a permanent conservation easement if the state of Minnesota or a political subdivision of the state owns the land in fee or if the land is wholly or partially subject to a conservation easement.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the purchase creates additional direct benefit to protect, restore, or enhance the state's wetlands, prairies, forests, or habitat for fish, game, and wildlife, and the purchase is approved by an affirmative vote of at least nine members of the council; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the purchase is for land that is partially subject to a conservation easement and no money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund is used to pay the purchase price for the portion of land that is subject to the easement. Nothing in this clause prohibits the use of money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund to pay for costs and other expenses associated with the acquisition of the land as part of the larger acquisition.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of this subdivision, "conservation easement" means a conservation easement as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84C.01">84C.01</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Restoration and enhancement evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources and the Board of Water and Soil Resources must convene a technical evaluation panel comprised of five members, including one technical representative from the Board of Water and Soil Resources, one technical representative from the Department of Natural Resources, one technical expert from the University of Minnesota or the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, and two representatives with expertise in the project being evaluated. The board and the commissioner may add a technical representative from a unit of federal or local government.</p>
          <p>(b) The members of the technical evaluation panel may not be associated with the restoration or enhancement, may vary depending upon the projects being reviewed, and shall avoid any potential conflicts of interest.</p>
          <p>(c) Each year, the board and the commissioner may assign a coordinator to identify habitat restoration or enhancement projects completed with outdoor heritage funding. The coordinator shall secure the plans for the projects specified and direct the technical evaluation panel to evaluate the restorations and enhancements relative to the law, current science, and the stated goals and standards in the project plan and, when applicable, to the Board of Water and Soil Resources' Native Vegetation Establishment and Enhancement Guidelines.</p>
          <p>(d) The coordinator shall summarize the findings of the panel and provide a report to the chair of the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council and the chairs of the respective house of representatives and senate policy and finance committees with jurisdiction over natural resources and spending from the outdoor heritage fund. The report shall determine if the restorations and enhancements are meeting planned goals, any problems with the implementation of restorations and enhancements, and, if necessary, recommendations on improving restorations and enhancements. The report shall be focused on improving future restorations and enhancements.</p>
          <p>(e) At least one-tenth of one percent of forecasted receipts from the outdoor heritage fund must be used for restoration and enhancements evaluations under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Recipient requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A state agency or other recipient of a direct appropriation from the outdoor heritage fund must compile and submit all information for funded projects or programs, including the proposed measurable outcomes and all other items required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>, to the Legislative Coordinating Commission as soon as practicable or by January 15 of the applicable fiscal year, whichever comes first. The Legislative Coordinating Commission must post submitted information on the website required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>, as soon as it becomes available.</p>
          <p>(b) When practicable, a direct recipient of an appropriation from the outdoor heritage fund shall prominently display on the recipient's website home page the legacy logo required under Laws 2009, chapter 172, article 5, section 10, as amended by Laws 2010, chapter 361, article 3, section 5, accompanied by the phrase "Click here for more information." When a person clicks on the legacy logo image, the website must direct the person to a web page that includes both the contact information that a person may use to obtain additional information, as well as a link to the Legislative Coordinating Commission website required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) Future eligibility for money from the outdoor heritage fund is contingent upon a state agency or other recipient satisfying all applicable requirements in this section, as well as any additional requirements contained in applicable session law. If the Office of the Legislative Auditor, in the course of an audit or investigation, publicly reports that a recipient of money from the outdoor heritage fund has not complied with the laws, rules, or regulations in this section or other laws applicable to the recipient, the recipient must be listed in an annual report to the legislative committees with jurisdiction over the legacy funds. The list must be publicly available. The legislative auditor shall remove a recipient from the list upon determination that the recipient is in compliance. A recipient on the list is not eligible for future funding from the outdoor heritage fund until the recipient demonstrates compliance to the legislative auditor.</p>
          <p>(d) A project or program receiving funding from the outdoor heritage fund must include an assessment of whether the funding celebrates cultural diversity or reaches diverse communities in Minnesota, including reaching low- and moderate-income households.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Accomplishment plans.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a condition of acceptance of money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund that the agency or entity using the appropriation submits an accomplishment plan and periodic accomplishment reports to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council in the form determined by the council. The accomplishment plan must identify the project manager responsible for expending the appropriation and the final product. The accomplishment plan must account for the use of the appropriation and outcomes of the expenditure in measures of wetlands, prairies, forests, and fish, game, and wildlife habitat restored, protected, and enhanced. The plan must include an evaluation of results. If lands are acquired by fee with money from the outdoor heritage fund, the accomplishment plan must include a hunting and fishing management plan for the lands acquired by fee. No money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund may be expended unless the council has approved the pertinent accomplishment plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Project requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As a condition of accepting money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund, an agency or entity receiving money from an appropriation must comply with this subdivision for any project funded in whole or in part with funds from the appropriation.</p>
          <p>(b) All conservation easements acquired with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund must:</p>
          <p>(1) be permanent;</p>
          <p>(2) specify the parties to the easement;</p>
          <p>(3) specify all of the provisions of an agreement that are permanent;</p>
          <p>(4) specify the habitat types and location being protected;</p>
          <p>(5) where appropriate for conservation or water protection outcomes, require the grantor to employ practices retaining water on the eased land as long as practicable;</p>
          <p>(6) specify the responsibilities of the parties for habitat enhancement and restoration and the associated costs of these activities;</p>
          <p>(7) be sent to the office of the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council;</p>
          <p>(8) include a long-term stewardship plan and identify the sources and amount of funding for monitoring and enforcing the easement agreement; and</p>
          <p>(9) identify the parties responsible for monitoring and enforcing the easement agreement.</p>
          <p>(c) For all restorations, a recipient must prepare and retain an ecological restoration and management plan that, to the degree practicable, is consistent with current conservation science and ecological goals for the restoration site. Consideration should be given to soil, geology, topography, and other relevant factors that would provide the best chance for long-term success and durability of the restoration. The plan must include the proposed timetable for implementing the restoration, including, but not limited to, site preparation, establishment of diverse plant species, maintenance, and additional enhancement to establish the restoration; identify long-term maintenance and management needs of the restoration and how the maintenance, management, and enhancement will be financed; and use current conservation science to achieve the best restoration.</p>
          <p>(d) For new lands acquired, a recipient must prepare a restoration and management plan in compliance with paragraph (c), including identification of sufficient funding for implementation.</p>
          <p>(e) To ensure public accountability for the use of public funds, a recipient must provide to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council documentation of the process used to select parcels acquired in fee or as permanent conservation easements and must provide the council with documentation of all related transaction costs, including, but not limited to, appraisals, legal fees, recording fees, commissions, other similar costs, and donations. This information must be provided for all parties involved in the transaction. The recipient must also report to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council any difference between the acquisition amount paid to the seller and the state-certified or state-reviewed appraisal, if a state-certified or state-reviewed appraisal was conducted. The commissioner of natural resources may conduct or require additional appraisals of parcels to be acquired in fee title or as conservation easements. Acquisition data such as appraisals may remain private during negotiations but must ultimately be made public according to chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13">13</a>.</p>
          <p>(f) Except as otherwise provided in the appropriation, all restoration and enhancement projects funded with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund must be on land permanently protected by a conservation easement or public ownership or in public waters as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.15">103G.005, subdivision 15</a>.</p>
          <p>(g) To the extent an appropriation is used to acquire an interest in real property, a recipient of an appropriation from the outdoor heritage fund must provide to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council and the commissioner of management and budget an analysis of increased operation and maintenance costs likely to be incurred by public entities as a result of the acquisition and of how the costs are to be paid.</p>
          <p>(h) A recipient of money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund must give consideration to and make timely written contact with Conservation Corps Minnesota for possible use of the corps' services to contract for restoration and enhancement services. A copy of the written contact must be filed with the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council within 15 days of execution.</p>
          <p>(i) A recipient of money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund must erect signage according to Laws 2009, chapter 172, article 5, section 10.</p>
          <p>(j) At least 30 days before closing on an acquisition of land in fee title with money in whole or in part from the outdoor heritage fund, a nongovernmental organization must notify in writing the county board and town board where the land is located and furnish them a description of the land to be acquired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Purchase of recycled and recyclable materials.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision, public or private corporation, or other entity that receives money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund must use the money in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.0725">16C.0725</a>, regarding purchase of recycled, repairable, and durable materials, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.073">16C.073</a>, regarding purchase and use of paper stock and printing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Land acquisition restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An interest in real property, including, but not limited to, an easement or fee title, that is acquired with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund must be used in perpetuity or for the specific term of an easement interest for the purpose for which the appropriation was made. The ownership of the interest in real property transfers to the state if:</p>
          <p>(1) the holder of the interest in real property fails to comply with the terms and conditions of the grant agreement or accomplishment plan; or</p>
          <p>(2) restrictions are placed on the land that preclude its use for the intended purpose as specified in the appropriation.</p>
          <p>(b) A recipient of funding that acquires an interest in real property subject to this subdivision may not alter the intended use of the interest in real property or convey any interest in the real property acquired with the appropriation without the prior review and approval of the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council or its successor. The council shall notify the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over the outdoor heritage fund at least 15 business days before approval under this paragraph. The council shall establish procedures to review requests from recipients to alter the use of or convey an interest in real property. These procedures shall allow for the replacement of the interest in real property with another interest in real property meeting the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the interest must be at least equal in fair market value, as certified by the commissioner of natural resources, to the interest being replaced; and</p>
          <p>(2) the interest must be in a reasonably equivalent location and have a reasonably equivalent useful conservation purpose compared to the interest being replaced, taking into consideration all effects from fragmentation of the whole habitat.</p>
          <p>(c) A recipient of funding who acquires an interest in real property under paragraph (a) must separately record a notice of funding restrictions in the appropriate local government office where the conveyance of the interest in real property is filed. The notice of funding agreement must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) a legal description of the interest in real property covered by the funding agreement;</p>
          <p>(2) a reference to the underlying funding agreement;</p>
          <p>(3) a reference to this section; and</p>
          <p>(4) the following statement: "This interest in real property shall be administered in accordance with the terms, conditions, and purposes of the grant agreement controlling the acquisition of the property. The interest in real property, or any portion of the interest in real property, shall not be sold, transferred, pledged, or otherwise disposed of or further encumbered without obtaining the prior written approval of the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council or its successor. The ownership of the interest in real property transfers to the state if: (1) the holder of the interest in real property fails to comply with the terms and conditions of the grant agreement or accomplishment plan; or (2) restrictions are placed on the land that preclude its use for the intended purpose as specified in the appropriation."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Real property interest report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By December 1 each year, a recipient of money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund that is used for the acquisition of an interest in real property, including, but not limited to, an easement or fee title, must submit annual reports on the status of the real property to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council or its successor in a form determined by the council. If lands are acquired by fee with money from the outdoor heritage fund, the real property interest report must include a verification of the status of the hunting and fishing management plan for the lands acquired by fee. The responsibility for reporting under this subdivision may be transferred by the recipient of the appropriation to another person or entity that holds the interest in the real property. To complete the transfer of reporting responsibility, the recipient of the appropriation must:</p>
          <p>(1) inform the person to whom the responsibility is transferred of that person's reporting responsibility;</p>
          <p>(2) inform the person to whom the responsibility is transferred of the property restrictions under subdivision 15; and</p>
          <p>(3) provide written notice to the council of the transfer of reporting responsibility, including contact information for the person to whom the responsibility is transferred.</p>
          <p>(b) After the transfer, the person or entity that holds the interest in the real property is responsible for reporting requirements under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Easement monitoring and enforcement requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund for easement monitoring and enforcement may be spent only on activities included in an easement monitoring and enforcement plan contained within the accomplishment plan. Money received for monitoring and enforcement, including earnings on the money received, shall be kept in a monitoring and enforcement fund held by the organization and is appropriated for monitoring and enforcing conservation easements in the state. Within 120 days after the close of the entity's fiscal year, an entity receiving appropriations for easement monitoring and enforcement must provide an annual financial report to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council on the easement monitoring and enforcement fund as specified in the accomplishment plan. Money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund for monitoring and enforcement of easements and earnings on the money appropriated shall revert to the state if:</p>
          <p>(1) the easement transfers to the state under subdivision 15;</p>
          <p>(2) the holder of the easement fails to file an annual report and then fails to cure that default within 30 days of notification of the default by the state; or</p>
          <p>(3) the holder of the easement fails to comply with the terms of the monitoring and enforcement plan contained within the accomplishment plan and fails to cure that default within 90 days of notification of the default by the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Successor organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>The Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council may approve the continuation of a project with an organization that has adopted a new name. Continuation of a project with an organization that has undergone a significant change in mission, structure, or purpose requires:</p>
          <p>(1) notice to the chairs of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over the outdoor heritage fund; and</p>
          <p>(2) presentation by the council of proposed legislation either ratifying or rejecting continued involvement with the new organization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Fee title acquisition; open season.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Lands acquired by fee with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund that are held by the state must be open to the public taking of fish and game during the open season, unless otherwise provided by state law.</p>
          <p>(b) Lands acquired by fee with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund that are held by the United States Fish and Wildlife Service must be open to the public taking of fish and game during the open season according to the National Wildlife Refuge System Improvement Act, United States Code, title 16, section 668dd, et seq.</p>
          <p>(c) Except as provided in paragraph (b), lands acquired by fee with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund that are held by a nonstate entity must be open to the public taking of fish and game during the open season, unless otherwise prescribed by the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Donations.</span></h2>
          <p>A recipient shall not accept a monetary donation or payment from an owner of land that is acquired in fee in whole or in part with an appropriation from the outdoor heritage fund that exceeds the documented expenses that are directly related to and necessary for activities specified in the accomplishment plan approved by the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council, unless expressly approved by the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council in the accomplishment plan. This subdivision does not apply to donations that are not connected with the acquisition transaction or bargain sales, as defined by Code of Federal Regulations, title 26, section 1.1011-2, provided that the purchase price reimbursed by the state does not exceed the purchase price paid by the recipient.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Haying and grazing.</span></h2>
          <p>Lands acquired with money appropriated from the outdoor heritage fund may not be used for emergency haying and grazing in response to federal or state disaster declarations. Conservation grazing under a management plan that is being implemented prior to the emergency declaration may continue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Revenues.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A recipient must disclose to the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council and the commissioner all revenues that are received by the recipient before the availability of the appropriation ends and that are generated from activities on land acquired in fee title or easement, restored, or enhanced with money from the outdoor heritage fund. The revenues must be disclosed to the council and commissioner no later than 90 days after the availability of the appropriation ends.</p>
          <p>(b) For all revenues disclosed under paragraph (a), a recipient must:</p>
          <p>(1) use the revenues to protect, restore, or enhance wetlands, prairies, forests, or habitat for fish, game, or wildlife according to the appropriation purposes and the approved accomplishment plan;</p>
          <p>(2) use the revenues for other purposes as approved in the accomplishment plan by the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council; or</p>
          <p>(3) transfer the revenues to the outdoor heritage fund no later than 90 days after the availability of the appropriation ends, unless otherwise approved by the council.</p>
          <p>(c) Paragraph (b), clause (3), does not apply to the state and its departments and agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Reserve requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>In any fiscal year, at least five percent of that year's projected tax receipts determined by the most recent forecast for the outdoor heritage fund must not be appropriated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.056.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Previous funding; notification requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Any state agency or organization requesting a direct appropriation from the outdoor heritage fund must inform the Lessard-Sams Outdoor Heritage Council and the house of representatives and senate committees having jurisdiction over the outdoor heritage fund, at the time the request for funding is made, whether the request is supplanting or is a substitution for any previous funding that was not from a legacy fund and was used for the same purpose.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.056.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.14.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/172/#laws.1.3.0">2009 c 172 art 1 s 3</a>-6,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.1.4.0">2010 c 361 art 1 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/6/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp2011 c 6 art 1 s 4</a>-9; art 5 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/264/#laws.1.3.0">2012 c 264 art 1 s 3</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.5.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/256/#laws.1.3.0">2014 c 256 art 1 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/2/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp2015 c 2 art 1 s 4</a>-6; art 5 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/172/#laws.1.3.0">2016 c 172 art 1 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/91/#laws.1.3.0">2017 c 91 art 1 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/208/#laws.0.3.0">2018 c 208 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/104/#laws.1.3.0">2020 c 104 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/1/#laws.1.3.0">1Sp2021 c 1 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/40/#laws.1.3.0">2023 c 40 art 1 s 3</a>-5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.057">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.057 FEDERAL LAW COMPLIANCE; RESTRICTION ON LICENSE REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.057.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Compliance with federal law.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall take any action necessary to comply with the Federal Aid in Wildlife Restoration Act, United States Code, title 16, sections 669 to 669i, and the Federal Aid in Fish Restoration Act, United States Code, title 16, sections 777 to 777k. Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an appropriation for an information or telecommunications technology project from the game and fish fund, as established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.055">97A.055</a>, must be made to the commissioner. Any assets acquired with or expenditures made from the game and fish fund must remain under control of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.057.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Restriction on license revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Money accruing to the state from fees charged for hunting and angling licenses shall not be used for any purpose other than game and fish activities and related activities under the administration of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.057.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/376/#laws.0.7.0">2002 c 376 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.11.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.15.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.97A.061"><b>97A.061</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.2.36.0">2013 c 143 art 2 s 36</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.065">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.065 DEDICATION OF CERTAIN RECEIPTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.065.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.91.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 91</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.065.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fines and forfeited bail.</span></h2>
          <p> Fines and forfeited bail collected from prosecutions of violations of: the game and fish laws or rules adopted thereunder; sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.091">84.091</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.15">84.15</a> or rules adopted thereunder; chapter 348; and any other law relating to wild animals or aquatic vegetation, must be deposited in the state treasury. Half the receipts must be credited to the general fund, and half the receipts must be credited to the game and fish fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.055">97A.055</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.065.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.28.0">1994 c 561 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.065.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.54.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 54</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.065.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Restitution for wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>Money collected from restitution under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.341">97A.341</a> for wild animals killed, injured, or possessed in violation of the game and fish laws must be used by the commissioner for replacement, propagation, or protection of wild animals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.065.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Deer license donations and surcharges.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The surcharges collected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.3a">97A.475, subdivision 3a</a>, paragraph (b), shall be deposited in an account in the special revenue fund and are appropriated to the commissioner for deer management, including for grants or payments to agencies, organizations, or individuals for assisting with the cost of processing deer taken for population management purposes for venison donation programs. None of the additional license fees shall be transferred to any other agency for administration of programs other than venison donation. If any money transferred by the commissioner is not used for a venison donation program, it shall be returned to the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The surcharges and donations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475, subdivisions 3</a>, paragraph (b); 3a, paragraph (a); and 4, paragraph (b), shall be deposited in an account in the special revenue fund and are appropriated to the commissioner for the walk-in access program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.065.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.12.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/429/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 429 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.8.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.1.5.0">1987 c 384 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.118.0">1987 c 404 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/19/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 19 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/298/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 298 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/184/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 184 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.58.0">1996 c 410 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.7.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/367/#laws.8.1.0">1998 c 367 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.11.1.0">1999 c 243 art 11 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.29.0">2000 c 495 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.23.0">2001 c 185 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.5.1.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.13.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.18.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.81.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 81</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.21.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.3.6.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 3 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.071">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.071 WILDLIFE ACQUISITION ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.071.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established.</span></h2>
          <p>The wildlife acquisition account is established as an account in the game and fish fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.071.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Revenue from small-game license surcharge and lifetime licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>Revenue from the small-game surcharge and $6.50 annually from the lifetime fish and wildlife trust fund, established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.4742">97A.4742</a>, for each license issued under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a>, subdivisions 3 and 5, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.474#stat.97A.474.3">97A.474, subdivision 3</a>, shall be credited to the wildlife acquisition account and is appropriated to the commissioner. The money in the account shall be used by the commissioner only for the purposes of this section, and acquisition and development of wildlife lands under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a> and maintenance of the lands, in accordance with appropriations made by the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.071.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Use of wildlife acquisition account money.</span></h2>
          <p>Of the money annually appropriated and available from the wildlife acquisition account:</p>
          <p>(1) at least 50 percent must be used for land costs; and</p>
          <p>(2) the remainder may only be used for other land acquisition costs, development, and maintenance of wildlife lands, and activities under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.071.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Waterfowl breeding grounds in Canada.</span></h2>
          <p>The wildlife acquisition account may be used for developing, preserving, restoring, and maintaining waterfowl breeding grounds in Canada under agreement or contract with any nonprofit organization dedicated to the construction, maintenance, and repair of projects that are acceptable to the governmental agency having jurisdiction over the land and water affected by the projects. The commissioner may execute agreements and contracts if the commissioner determines that the use of the funds will benefit the migration of waterfowl into the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.071.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.28.0">1994 c 561 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.071.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Development" means fencing, signing, and on-site improvement of the land that is related to the purposes for which the land was acquired. Development includes material or equipment that is purchased or rented and labor that is necessary to provide for the onsite improvement of the land.</p>
          <p>(c) "Land costs" means the purchase price of land acquired by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Maintenance" means noxious weed control and other on-site functions performed on a regular basis to sustain the environmental conditions that result from the original improvement of the land.</p>
          <p>(e) "Other acquisition costs" means acquisition coordination costs, costs of engineering services, appraisal fees, attorney fees, taxes, assessments required at the time of purchase, and recording fees for land acquired by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.071.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.13.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.22.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.53.0">1993 c 172 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.6.0">1994 c 561 s 6</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/341/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 341 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.50.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.11.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.075">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.075 USE OF LICENSE REVENUES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Deer, bear, and lifetime licenses.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "deer license" means a license issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475, subdivisions 2, clauses</a> (5), (6), (7), (15), (16), and (17); 3, paragraph (a), clauses (2), (3), (4), (12), (13), and (14); and 8, paragraph (b), and licenses issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.301#stat.97B.301.4">97B.301, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The deer management account is established as an account in the game and fish fund and may be used only for deer habitat improvement or deer management programs, including a computerized licensing system. The following amounts must be credited to the deer management account:</p>
          <p>(1) $16 from each annual deer license issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475, subdivisions 2</a>, clauses (5), (6), and (7); 3, paragraph (a), clauses (2), (3), and (4); and 8, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(2) $2 from each annual deer license issued under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475, subdivisions 2</a>, clauses (15), (16), and (17); and 3, paragraph (a), clauses (12), (13), and (14); and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.301">97B.301</a>, subdivision 4; and</p>
          <p>(3) $16 annually from the lifetime fish and wildlife trust fund, established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.4742">97A.4742</a>, for each license issued to a person 18 years of age or older under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473#stat.97A.473.4">97A.473, subdivision 4</a>, and $2 annually from the lifetime fish and wildlife trust fund for each license issued to a person under 18 years of age.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) $1 from each annual deer license and each bear license and $1 annually from the lifetime fish and wildlife trust fund, established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.4742">97A.4742</a>, for each license issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473#stat.97A.473.4">97A.473, subdivision 4</a>, must be credited to the deer and bear management account and is appropriated to the commissioner for deer- and bear-management programs, including a computerized licensing system.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Fifty cents from each deer license is credited to the emergency deer feeding and wild Cervidae health-management account and is appropriated for emergency deer feeding and wild Cervidae health management. Money appropriated for emergency deer feeding and wild Cervidae health management is available until expended.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) When the unencumbered balance in the appropriation for emergency deer feeding and wild Cervidae health management exceeds $2,500,000 at the end of a fiscal year, the unencumbered balance over $2,500,000 is canceled and is available for deer- and bear-management programs and computerized licensing.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Minnesota migratory-waterfowl stamp.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Ninety percent of the revenue from the Minnesota migratory-waterfowl stamps must be credited to the waterfowl habitat improvement account and is appropriated to the commissioner only for:</p>
          <p>(1) development of wetlands and lakes in the state and designated waterfowl management lakes for maximum migratory waterfowl production including habitat evaluation, the construction of dikes, water control structures and impoundments, nest cover, common carp barriers, acquisition of sites and facilities necessary for development and management of existing migratory waterfowl habitat and the designation of waters under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.101">97A.101</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) management of migratory waterfowl;</p>
          <p>(3) development, restoration, maintenance, or preservation of migratory waterfowl habitat;</p>
          <p>(4) acquisition of and access to structure sites; and</p>
          <p>(5) the promotion of waterfowl habitat development and maintenance, including promotion and evaluation of government farm program benefits for waterfowl habitat.</p>
          <p>(b) Money in the account may not be used for costs unless they are directly related to a specific parcel of land or body of water under paragraph (a), clause (1), (3), (4), or (5), or to specific management activities under paragraph (a), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Trout-and-salmon stamp.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Ninety percent of the revenue from trout-and-salmon stamps must be credited to the trout and salmon management account and is appropriated to the commissioner only for:</p>
          <p>(1) the development, restoration, maintenance, improvement, protection, and preservation of habitat for trout and salmon in trout streams and lakes, including, but not limited to, evaluating habitat; stabilizing eroding stream banks; adding fish cover; modifying stream channels; managing vegetation to protect, shade, or reduce runoff on stream banks; and purchasing equipment to accomplish these tasks;</p>
          <p>(2) rearing trout and salmon, including utility and service costs associated with coldwater hatchery buildings and systems; stocking trout and salmon in streams and lakes and Lake Superior; and monitoring and evaluating stocked trout and salmon;</p>
          <p>(3) acquisition of easements and fee title along trout waters;</p>
          <p>(4) identifying easement and fee title areas along trout waters; and</p>
          <p>(5) research and special management projects on trout streams, trout lakes, and Lake Superior and portions of its tributaries.</p>
          <p>(b) Money in the account may not be used for costs unless they are directly related to a specific parcel of land or body of water under paragraph (a), to specific fish rearing activities under paragraph (a), clause (2), or for costs associated with supplies and equipment to implement trout and salmon management activities under paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Pheasant stamp.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Ninety percent of the revenue from pheasant stamps must be credited to the pheasant habitat improvement account and is appropriated to the commissioner only for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the development, restoration, and maintenance of suitable habitat for ringnecked pheasants on public and private land including the establishment of nesting cover, winter cover, and reliable food sources;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) reimbursement of landowners for setting aside lands for pheasant habitat;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) reimbursement of expenditures to provide pheasant habitat on public and private land;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the promotion of pheasant habitat development and maintenance, including promotion and evaluation of government farm program benefits for pheasant habitat; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the acquisition of lands suitable for pheasant habitat management and public hunting.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Money in the account may not be used for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) costs unless they are directly related to a specific parcel of land under paragraph (a), clause (1), (3), or (5), or to specific promotional or evaluative activities under paragraph (a), clause (4); or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) any personnel costs, except that prior to July 1, 2019, personnel may be hired to provide technical and promotional assistance for private landowners to implement conservation provisions of state and federal programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Turkey account.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) $4.50 from each turkey license sold, except youth licenses under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.2">97A.475, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4), and subdivision 3, paragraph (a), clause (7), must be credited to the wild-turkey management account and is appropriated to the commissioner only for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the development, restoration, and maintenance of suitable habitat for wild turkeys on public and private land including forest stand improvement and establishment of nesting cover, winter roost area, and reliable food sources;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) acquisitions of, or easements on, critical wild-turkey habitat;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) reimbursement of expenditures to provide wild-turkey habitat on public and private land;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) trapping and transplantation of wild turkeys; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the promotion of turkey habitat development and maintenance, population surveys and monitoring, and research.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Money in the account may not be used for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) costs unless they are directly related to a specific parcel of land under paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (3), a specific trap and transplant project under paragraph (a), clause (4), or to specific promotional or evaluative activities under paragraph (a), clause (5); or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) any permanent personnel costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Walleye stamp.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> Revenue from walleye stamps must be credited to the walleye stamp account and is appropriated to the commissioner only for stocking walleyes purchased from the private sector in waters of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.075.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Wolf licenses; account established.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "wolf license" means a license or permit issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.2">97A.475, subdivision 2</a>, clause (22); 3, paragraph (a), clause (18); or 20, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) A wolf management and monitoring account is created in the game and fish fund. Revenue from wolf licenses must be credited to the wolf management and monitoring account and is appropriated to the commissioner only for wolf management, research, damage control, enforcement, and education. Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, money credited to the account may not be used to pay indirect costs or agency shared services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.075.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.14.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.9.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.24.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.54.0">1993 c 172 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.10.0">1994 c 561 s 10</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/294/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 294 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/364/#laws.0.3.0">1996 c 364 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.80.0">1997 c 216 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.16.0">1997 c 226 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/249/#laws.0.11.0">1999 c 249 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/341/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 341 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/376/#laws.0.8.0">2002 c 376 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.51.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 51</a>-53; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.97.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.19.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.15.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 15</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.33.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.12.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.12.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 12</a>; art 5 s 21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.22.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 22</a>,23; art 2 s 2,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.3.36.0">2016 c 189 art 3 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.9.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.70.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.48.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.50.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 50</a>,51; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.2.12.0">2024 c 90 art 2 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
