      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 1 by Laws 2017, chapter 93, article 2, section 70, is effective July 1 of the year following the year the wolf is delisted under the federal Endangered Species Act. Laws 2017, chapter 93, article 2, section 70, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.97A.081"><b>97A.081</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.70.0">2014 c 290 s 70</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.97A.083"><b>97A.083</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.70.0">2014 c 290 s 70</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.085">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.085 GAME REFUGES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State parks.</span></h2>
          <p>All state parks are designated as game refuges.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate a game refuge if more than 50 percent of the area is in public ownership. The game refuge must be a contiguous area of at least 640 acres unless it borders or includes a marsh, or other body of water or watercourse suitable for wildlife habitat.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Establishment by petition of land holders.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate a land area or portion of a land area described in a petition as a game refuge. The petition must be signed by the owner, the lessee, or the person in possession of each tract in the area. A certificate of the auditor of the county where the lands are located must accompany the petition stating that the persons named in the petition are the owners, lessees, or persons in possession of all of the land described according to the county records. The game refuge must be a contiguous area of at least 640 acres unless it borders or includes a marsh, or other body of water or watercourse suitable for wildlife habitat.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Establishment by petition of county residents.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate as a game refuge public waters or a contiguous area described in a petition, signed by 50 or more residents of the county where the public waters or area is located. The game refuge must be a contiguous area of at least 640 acres unless it borders or includes a marsh, or other body of water or watercourse suitable for wildlife habitat. The game refuge may be designated only if the commissioner finds that protected wild animals are depleted and are in danger of extermination, or that it will best serve the public interest. If any of the land area in the proposed game refuge is privately owned and the commissioner receives a petition opposing designation of the refuge signed by the owners, lessees, or persons in possession of at least 75 percent of the private land area within the proposed game refuge, the commissioner shall not designate the private lands as a game refuge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Hearing required.</span></h2>
          <p>Before designating a game refuge under this section, the commissioner must hold a public hearing within the county where the majority of the proposed game refuge exists. Notices of the time and place of the hearing must be posted in five conspicuous places within the proposed game refuge at least 15 days before the hearing. A notice of the hearing must be published in a legal newspaper in each county where the area is located at least seven days before the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Game refuge for specified game.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate a game refuge under this section for only specified species. The game refuge must be posted accordingly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Area included in game refuge.</span></h2>
          <p>A state game refuge includes all public lands, waters, highways, and railroad right-of-way within the refuge boundary and, in the discretion of the commissioner, may include adjacent public lands and waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Game refuge boundary posting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The designation of a state game refuge is not effective until the boundary has been posted with notices that measure at least 12 inches.</p>
          <p>(b) The notices must be posted at intervals of not more than 500 feet or less along the boundary. The notices must also be posted at all public road entrances to the refuges, except where the boundary is also an international or state boundary in public waters. Where the boundary of a refuge extends more than 500 feet continuously through a body of water, instead of placing notices in the water, notices with the words, "Adjacent Waters Included," may be placed on the shoreline at the intersection of the boundary and the water 20 feet or less above the high-water mark and at intervals of 500 feet or less along the shoreline.</p>
          <p>(c) A certification by the commissioner or the director of the Wildlife Division, or a certification filed with the commissioner or director by a conservation officer, refuge supervisor, or other authorized officer or employee, stating that the required notices have been posted is prima facie evidence of the posting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Modification or abandonment.</span></h2>
          <p>A state game refuge may be vacated or modified by the commissioner under the same procedures required for establishment of the refuge, except that a refuge established or modified under subdivision 2 or 3 may be vacated or modified following a public hearing as specified in subdivision 4a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.085.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Vacating refuges open to hunting.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 8, the commissioner may vacate a state game refuge by publishing a notice in the State Register if the refuge has been open to trapping and hunting small game including waterfowl, deer or bear by archery, and deer or bear by firearms for at least five years.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.085.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.16.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.10.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/462/#laws.0.6.0">1992 c 462 s 6</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.3.23.0">1997 c 187 art 3 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 226 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/323/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 323 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/215/#laws.0.2.0">2004 c 215 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.20.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.24.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.091">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.091 HUNTING ON GAME REFUGES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.091.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hunting and possession of firearms.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 2, a person may not take a wild animal, except fish, within a state game refuge. A person may not carry within a refuge:</p>
          <p>(1) a firearm unless the firearm is unloaded and contained in a case, or unloaded and broken down; or</p>
          <p>(2) an uncased bow.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.091.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">When hunting allowed.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may allow hunting of a protected wild animal species within any portion of a state game refuge, including a state park. Hunting may be allowed under this paragraph only if the commissioner finds:</p>
          <p>(1) the population of the species exceeds the refuge's carrying capacity;</p>
          <p>(2) the species is causing substantial damage to agricultural or forest crops in the vicinity;</p>
          <p>(3) the species or other protected wild animals are threatened by the species population; or</p>
          <p>(4) a harvestable surplus of the species exists.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may allow hunting of unprotected wild animals in a game refuge.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may prescribe rules for any hunting allowed within a refuge.</p>
          <p>(d) In any selection process for permits to take deer within a game refuge, the commissioner may designate a certain number of permits that are available only to applicants who are age 70 or over or are qualified for a special permit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.055#stat.97B.055.3">97B.055, subdivision 3</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.106">97B.106</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.091.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Trap or target shooting.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue special permits, without fee, to the owner or lessee of privately owned land within the boundaries of a state game refuge for trap or target shooting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.091.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.17.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.8.0">1993 c 231 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.13.0">1994 c 561 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.092">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.092 CONTROLLED HUNTING ZONES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may by rule establish controlled hunting zones in areas on or adjacent to wildlife management areas and game refuges where the commissioner determines it is necessary to limit the distribution of waterfowl hunters. The commissioner may by rule establish conditions for entry and restrictions on hunting in a controlled hunting zone, including procedures for impartially selecting hunters for the zone.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.092.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.10.0">1993 c 231 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.093">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.093 HUNTING, TRAPPING, AND FISHING IN SCIENTIFIC AND NATURAL AREAS.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided by law, scientific and natural areas are closed to hunting, trapping, and fishing unless:</p>
        <p>(1) the designating document allows hunting, trapping, or fishing; or</p>
        <p>(2) the commissioner allows hunting, trapping, or fishing in accordance with the procedure in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.5">86A.05, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (d).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.093.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.17.0">1991 c 259 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/462/#laws.0.12.0">1992 c 462 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/161/#laws.0.2.0">2005 c 161 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.095">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.095 WATERFOWL PROTECTED AREAS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.095.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Migratory-waterfowl sanctuary.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate by rule any part of a state game refuge or any part of a public water that is designated for management purposes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.101#stat.97A.101.2">97A.101, subdivision 2</a>, as a migratory-waterfowl sanctuary. The commissioner must consider an area for designation upon presentation of a petition signed by at least ten residents demonstrating that the area is primarily a migratory-waterfowl refuge. The commissioner shall post the area as a migratory-waterfowl sanctuary. A person may not enter a posted migratory-waterfowl sanctuary during the open migratory-waterfowl season or during other times prescribed by the commissioner unless accompanied by or under a permit issued by a conservation officer or wildlife manager. Upon a request from a private landowner within a migratory-waterfowl sanctuary, an annual permit must be issued to provide access to the property during the waterfowl season. The permit shall include conditions that allow no activity which would disturb waterfowl using the refuge during the waterfowl season.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.095.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Waterfowl feeding and resting areas.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may, by rule, designate any part of a lake as a migratory feeding and resting area if there is adequate, free public access to the area. Before designation, the commissioner must describe the area in a public notice and receive public comments for 30 days. The commissioner must consider an area for designation upon presentation of a petition signed by at least ten residents demonstrating that the area is a substantial feeding or resting area for migratory waterfowl. The commissioner shall post the area as a migratory-waterfowl feeding and resting area. Except as authorized in rules adopted by the commissioner, a person may not enter a posted migratory-waterfowl feeding and resting area, during a period when hunting of migratory waterfowl is allowed, with watercraft or aircraft propelled by a motor, other than an electric motor with battery power of 12 volts or less. The commissioner may, by rule, further restrict the use of electric motors in migratory-waterfowl feeding and resting areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.095.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.91.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 91</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.095.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Swan Lake Migratory-Waterfowl Sanctuary.</span></h2>
          <p>The land described in Laws 1999, chapter 81, section 2, is designated Swan Lake Migratory-Waterfowl Sanctuary under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.095.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.18.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/119/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 119 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.11.0">1993 c 231 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/81/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 81 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.30.0">2000 c 495 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/215/#laws.0.5.0">2004 c 215 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.14.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.25.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 25</a>,26</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.098">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.098 TRESPASS ON FEDERAL LANDS.</h1>
        <p>A person may not enter or use a national wildlife refuge or federal waterfowl production area in violation of federal law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.098.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.12.0">1993 c 231 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.101">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.101 PUBLIC WATER RESERVES AND MANAGEMENT DESIGNATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.101.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reserves.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate and reserve public waters of the state to propagate and protect wild animals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.101.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Management designation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may designate, reserve, and manage public waters for wildlife after giving notice and holding a public hearing. The hearing must be held in the county where the major portion of the waters is located. Notice of the hearing must be published in a legal newspaper within each county where the waters are located at least seven days before the hearing. The designation by the commissioner shall be by written order published in the State Register. Designations are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may contract with riparian owners for water projects under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.121#stat.103G.121.3">103G.121, subdivision 3</a>, and may acquire land, accept local funding, and construct, maintain, and operate structures to control water levels under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.505">103G.505</a> to manage designated waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.101.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Fishing may not be restricted.</span></h2>
          <p>Seasons or methods of taking fish other than minnows may not be restricted under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.101.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Restrictions on airboats, watercraft, and recreational vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The use of airboats is prohibited at all times on lakes designated for wildlife management purposes under this section unless otherwise authorized by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may restrict the use of motorized watercraft and recreational vehicles on lakes designated for wildlife management purposes by posting all public access points on the designated lake. To minimize disturbance to wildlife or to protect wildlife habitat, the commissioner may restrict the type of allowable motorized watercraft or recreational vehicle, horsepower or thrust of motor, speed of operation, and season or area of use. Designation of areas, times, and types of restrictions to be posted shall be by written order published in the State Register. Posting of the restrictions is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
          <p>(c) Before the commissioner establishes perpetual restrictions under paragraph (b), public comment must be received and a public meeting must be held in the county where the largest portion of the lake is located. Notice of the meeting must be published in a news release issued by the commissioner and in a newspaper of general circulation in the area where the waters are located. The notice must be published at least once between 30 and 60 days before the public meeting and at least once between seven and 30 days before the meeting. The notices required in this paragraph must summarize the proposed action, invite public comment, and specify a deadline for the receipt of public comments. The commissioner shall mail a copy of each required notice to persons who have registered their names with the commissioner for this purpose. The commissioner shall consider any public comments received in making a final decision. This paragraph does not apply to temporary restrictions that expire within 90 days of the effective date of the restrictions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.19.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.23.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.13.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.18.0">1997 c 226 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.37.0">2004 c 221 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.21.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.22.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.105">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.105 GAME FARMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may breed and propagate game birds, bear, or mute swans only on privately owned or leased land and after obtaining a license. Any of the permitted animals on a game farm may be sold to other licensed game farms. "Privately owned or leased land" includes waters that are shallow or marshy, are not actually navigable, and are not of substantial beneficial public use. Before an application for a license is considered, the applicant must enclose the area to sufficiently confine the animals to be raised in a manner approved by the commissioner. A license may be granted only if the commissioner finds the application is made in good faith with intention to actually carry on the business described in the application and the commissioner determines that the facilities are adequate for the business.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may purchase live game birds or their eggs without a license if the birds or eggs, or birds hatched from the eggs, are released into the wild, consumed, or processed for consumption within one year after they were purchased or hatched. This paragraph does not apply to the purchase of migratory waterfowl or their eggs.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not introduce mute swans into the wild without a permit issued by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer of license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A game farm license is transferable with the transfer of all or a portion of the title or leasehold of the land if:</p>
          <p>(1) the land transferred complies with the license requirements;</p>
          <p>(2) the land is used for the purposes of the license; and</p>
          <p>(3) a verified written report of the existing and intended land use is made to the commissioner, accompanied by a copy of deed, assignment, lease, or other instrument transferring the corresponding title or leasehold in the enclosed land.</p>
          <p>(b) A transfer of less than the whole interest in the license is not valid. Each bona fide partner or associate in the ownership or operation of a game farm must obtain a separate license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Ownership of wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>All wild animals and their offspring, of the species identified in the license, that are within the enclosure are the property of the game farm licensee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.176.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 176</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.176.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 176</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sale of live animals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A sale of live animals from a licensed game farm is not valid unless the animals are delivered to the purchaser or they are identified and kept separately.</p>
          <p>(b) Live animals sold through auction or through a broker are considered to be sold by the game farm licensee.</p>
          <p>(c) The sale agreement or contract must be in writing. The licensee must notify a purchaser of the death of an animal within 30 days and of the number of increase before July 20 of each year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Sale of products.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prescribe:</p>
          <p>(1) the manner that products of wild animals raised on game farms may be sold or transported; and</p>
          <p>(2) the tags or seals to be affixed to the products.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.6.2.0">2024 c 116 art 6 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.6.2.0">2024 c 116 art 6 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A licensee that does not comply with a provision of this section subjects all wild animals on the game farm to confiscation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.105.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules for:</p>
          <p>(1) issuing game farm licenses;</p>
          <p>(2) inspecting game farm facilities;</p>
          <p>(3) acquiring and disposing of game farm animals; and</p>
          <p>(4) record keeping and reporting by game farm licensees, including transactions handled by auction or broker.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.20.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/121/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 121 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.119.0">1987 c 404 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.13.0">1993 c 231 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.2.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.24.0">2001 c 185 s 24</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/373/#laws.0.28.0">2002 c 373 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.54.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.6.2.0">2024 c 116 art 6 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.106">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.106 FUR FARMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may breed and propagate fur-bearing animals only on privately owned or leased land and after obtaining a license. Any of the permitted animals on a fur farm may be sold to other licensed fur farms. "Privately owned or leased land" includes waters that are shallow or marshy, are not actually navigable, and are not of substantial beneficial public use. Before an application for a license is considered, the applicant must enclose the area to sufficiently confine the animals to be raised in a manner approved by the commissioner. A license may be granted only if the commissioner finds the application is made in good faith with intention to actually carry on the business described in the application and the commissioner determines that the facilities are adequate for the business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer of license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A fur farm license is transferable with the transfer of all or a portion of the title or leasehold of the land if:</p>
          <p>(1) the land transferred complies with the license requirements;</p>
          <p>(2) the land is used for the purposes of the license; and</p>
          <p>(3) a verified written report of the existing and intended land use is made to the commissioner, accompanied by a copy of deed, assignment, lease, or other instrument transferring the corresponding title or leasehold in the enclosed land.</p>
          <p>(b) A transfer of less than the whole interest in the license is not valid. Each bona fide partner or associate in the ownership or operation of a fur farm must obtain a separate license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">License fee.</span></h2>
          <p>For each fur farm, the owner must, on or before January 1, pay to the commissioner an annual fee of $250.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Fur farm account.</span></h2>
          <p>The fur farm account is established in the game and fish fund. Fees collected under this section and interest attributable to money in the account must be deposited in the account. Money in the account, including interest earned, is appropriated to the commissioner for administration and enforcement of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Ownership of wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>All wild animals and their offspring, of the species identified in the license, that are within the enclosure are the property of the fur farm licensee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Containment and disease control.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the Board of Animal Health and the commissioners of agriculture and health, must develop:</p>
          <p>(1) containment and disposal requirements for farmed fur-bearers; and</p>
          <p>(2) farmed fur-bearer disease testing and reporting requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Sale of live animals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A sale of live animals from a licensed fur farm is not valid unless the animals are delivered to the purchaser or they are identified and kept separately.</p>
          <p>(b) Live animals sold through auction or through a broker are considered to be sold by the fur farm licensee.</p>
          <p>(c) The sale agreement or contract must be in writing. The licensee must notify a purchaser of the death of an animal within 30 days and of the number of increase before July 20 of each year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Sale of pelts and products.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must prescribe:</p>
          <p>(1) the manner that pelts and products of wild animals raised on fur farms may be sold or transported; and</p>
          <p>(2) the tags or seals to be affixed to the pelts and products.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Fox and mink.</span></h2>
          <p>Fox and mink may not be bought or sold for breeding or propagating unless they have been pen-bred for at least two generations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Transporting live beaver.</span></h2>
          <p>Live beaver may not be transported without a permit from the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A licensee that does not comply with a provision of this section subjects all wild animals on the fur farm to confiscation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.106.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules for:</p>
          <p>(1) issuing fur farm licenses;</p>
          <p>(2) inspecting fur farm facilities;</p>
          <p>(3) acquiring fur farm animals; and</p>
          <p>(4) record keeping and reporting by fur farm licensees, including transactions handled by auction or broker.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.106.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.6.3.0">2024 c 116 art 6 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.97A.111"><b>97A.111</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.51.0">1997 c 226 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.115">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.115 SHOOTING PRESERVES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.115.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licenses; rules.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not operate a shooting preserve without a license. The commissioner may issue licenses to operate commercial shooting preserves and private shooting preserves if the commissioner determines that it is in the public interest and that there will not be an adverse effect on wild game bird populations. Private shooting preserves may only be located outside of the pheasant range as determined by the commissioner. The commissioner may adopt rules to implement this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.121">97A.121</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.115.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authorized species.</span></h2>
          <p>Species that may be released and hunted in a licensed shooting preserve must be specified in the license and are limited to unprotected birds, adult pheasant, and bobwhite quail for private shooting preserves and adult pheasant, bobwhite quail, turkey, mallard duck, black duck, and other species designated by the commissioner for commercial shooting preserves. These game birds must be pen hatched and raised.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.115.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Size of preserve.</span></h2>
          <p>A shooting preserve must be at least 40 but not more than 160 contiguous acres for private shooting preserves and at least 100 but not more than 1,000 contiguous acres, including any water area, for commercial shooting preserves.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.115.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Posting boundaries.</span></h2>
          <p>The boundaries of a shooting preserve must be clearly posted in a manner prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.115.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">License revocation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may revoke a shooting preserve license if the licensee or persons authorized to hunt in the preserve have been convicted of a violation under this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.121">97A.121</a>. After revocation, a new license may be issued in the discretion of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.115.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.22.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/605/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 605 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/623/#laws.1.22.0">1994 c 623 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.27.0">1995 c 186 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.121">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.121 HUNTING IN PRIVATE SHOOTING PRESERVES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hunter's license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person hunting released birds in a private shooting preserve must have the licenses required by law for hunting pheasants.</p>
          <p>(b) A license is not required to hunt authorized game birds on a commercial shooting preserve.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Season.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The open season for hunting in commercial shooting preserves is continuous. Sanctioned registered field trials in commercial shooting preserves may be held from April 16 to July 14 after notification to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The open season for hunting in a private shooting preserve is September 15 until December 31.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may restrict the open season after receiving a complaint, holding a public hearing, and finding that the population of wild game birds is in danger by hunting in the preserve.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Operator-established restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>A shooting preserve licensee may determine who is allowed to hunt in the preserve. In each preserve the licensee may establish the charge for taking game, the shooting hours, the season, limitations, and restrictions on the age, sex, and number of each species that may be taken by a hunter. These provisions may not conflict with this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.115">97A.115</a> and may not be less restrictive than any rule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Limits and identification.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 4a, the commissioner shall prescribe the minimum number of each authorized species that may be released and the percentage of each species that may be taken. The commissioner shall prescribe methods for identifying birds to be released.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Pheasants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A private shooting preserve licensed to release pheasants may release no more than 300 adult pheasants on the licensed shooting preserve area during the private shooting preserve hunting season. The number of pheasants harvested may not exceed 95 percent of the number of pheasants released.</p>
          <p>(b) A commercial shooting preserve must release at least 1,000 adult pheasants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Marking harvested game.</span></h2>
          <p>Harvested game, except ducks that are marked in accordance with regulations of the United States Fish and Wildlife Service, must be marked or identified by the shooting preserve in a manner prescribed by the commissioner. The commissioner may issue the tags or other markings at a cost of 15 cents each. The marking must remain attached on the bird while the bird is transported.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.121.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Record keeping.</span></h2>
          <p>A shooting preserve licensee must maintain a registration book listing the names, addresses, and hunting license numbers, if applicable, of all hunters, the date when they hunted, the amount and species of game taken, and the tag numbers or other markings affixed to each bird. A shooting preserve must keep records of the number of each species raised and purchased and the date and number of each species released. The records must be open to inspection by the commissioner at all reasonable times.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.23.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.14.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/588/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 588 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/605/#laws.0.2.0">1990 c 605 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.125">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.125 WILDLIFE HABITAT ON PRIVATE LAND.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may enter into agreements with landowners to develop or improve wildlife habitat on private land and provide financial, technical, and professional assistance and material.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.125.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.24.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 24</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.126">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.126 WALK-IN ACCESS PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.126.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A walk-in access program is established to provide public access to wildlife habitat on private land for hunting, bird-watching, nature photography, and similar compatible uses, excluding trapping, as provided under this section. The commissioner may enter into agreements with other units of government and landowners to provide private land hunting access.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.126.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of enrolled lands.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) From September 1 to May 31, a person must have a walk-in access validation in possession to hunt, photograph, and watch wildlife on private lands, including agricultural lands, that are posted as being enrolled in the walk-in access program.</p>
          <p>(b) Hunting, bird-watching, nature photography, and similar compatible uses on private lands that are posted as enrolled in the walk-in access program is allowed from one-half hour before sunrise to one-half hour after sunset.</p>
          <p>(c) Access on private lands that are posted as enrolled in the walk-in access program is restricted to nonmotorized use, except by persons with disabilities operating motor vehicles on established trails or field roads who possess a valid permit to shoot from a stationary vehicle under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.055#stat.97B.055.3">97B.055, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The general provisions for use of wildlife management areas adopted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.06">86A.06</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.137">97A.137</a>, relating to overnight use, alcoholic beverages, use of motorboats, firearms and target shooting, hunting stands, abandonment of trash and property, destruction or removal of property, introduction of plants or animals, and animal trespass, apply to use of lands enrolled in the walk-in access program.</p>
          <p>(e) Any use of enrolled lands other than use authorized under this section is prohibited, including:</p>
          <p>(1) harvesting bait, including minnows, leeches, and other live bait;</p>
          <p>(2) training dogs or using dogs for activities other than hunting; and</p>
          <p>(3) constructing or maintaining any building, dock, fence, billboard, sign, hunting blind, or other structure, unless constructed or maintained by the landowner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.126.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Walk-in-access validation; fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The fee for a walk-in-access validation is $3.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.126.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.27.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.52.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.42.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.127">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.127 FINANCING WATERFOWL DEVELOPMENT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may use funds appropriated for fish and wildlife programs for the purpose of developing, preserving, restoring, and maintaining waterfowl breeding grounds in Canada under agreement or contract with any nonprofit organization dedicated to the construction, maintenance, and repair of projects that are acceptable to the governmental agency having jurisdiction over the land and water affected by the projects. The commissioner may execute agreements and contracts if the commissioner determines that use of the funds will benefit the migration of waterfowl into the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.127.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/269/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 269 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.131">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.131 HATCHERIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may acquire property by gift, lease, purchase, or condemnation and may construct, maintain, operate, and alter facilities for hatcheries.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.131.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.25.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.16.0">2014 c 290 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.133">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.133 STATE WILDLIFE MANAGEMENT AREAS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.133.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>State wildlife management areas are established and designated as provided under this section. State wildlife management areas are located and named as indicated in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="history" id="stat.97A.133.history
			">
          <h2>History: </h2>
          <p class="first">
            <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 485 s 16</a>
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.133.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Payment in lieu of taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>The consolidated conservation lands included in state wildlife management areas are subject to the payment in lieu of tax as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.12">477A.12</a>, subdivision 1, clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="history" id="stat.97A.133.history
			">
          <h2>History: </h2>
          <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 485 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.10.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 10</a></p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.133.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">All-terrain vehicle travel within designated wildlife management areas.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On lands acquired by the state under chapter 84A that are designated after January 1, 1986, as wildlife management areas, the commissioner shall, by January 15, 2004, identify, designate, and sign at least 90 miles of all-terrain vehicle trails, not including public roads that are maintained and open to travel by other noncommercial vehicles, in corridors of disturbance that:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner determines are appropriate to connect trails, forest roads established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.71#stat.89.71.1">89.71, subdivision 1</a>, and public highways to provide reasonable travel for all-terrain vehicles; or</p>
          <p>(2) are areas of historic all-terrain vehicle use, including trails that end within a wildlife management area.</p>
          <p class="bl">The designated trails must be either within or contiguous to the wildlife management areas. The commissioner shall consult with wildlife management area users, including both motorized and nonmotorized trail users, in identifying and designating trails under this paragraph. Trail establishment must be in compliance with other state and federal law. Local governments and other trail sponsors may propose the designation of trails, including the designation as a grant-in-aid trail for the purposes of funding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.927#stat.84.927.2">84.927, subdivision 2</a>. Designation of trails by the commissioner, authorized under this subdivision, shall be by written order published in the State Register. Designations are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
          <p>(b) The following roads shall be open to travel by all-terrain vehicles when the roads are open to other noncommercial vehicles:</p>
          <p>(1) the Rapid River Forest Road, beginning at the west boundary of the Red Lake Wildlife Management Area at the southwest corner of Section 7, Township 156 North, Range 35 West, Beltrami County, thence in an easterly and northeasterly direction through the Red Lake Wildlife Management Area to the east boundary of the Red Lake Wildlife Management Area at the southwest corner of Section 7, Township 157 North, Range 33 West, Lake of the Woods County;</p>
          <p>(2) the Blanchard Forest Road, beginning at the junction of the North Shore Road along the northern shore of Upper Red Lake and the Blanchard State Forest Road at the west section line of Section 30, Township 155 North, Range 31 West, Beltrami County, thence in a westerly direction to the west section line of Section 31, Township 155 North, Range 32 West;</p>
          <p>(3) the Moose River Forest Road, beginning at the junction of Dick's Parkway State Forest Road and the Moose River State Forest Road at the southwest corner of Section 31, Township 36 West, Range 158 North, thence in a westerly direction along the Moose River State Forest Road to the junction of Beltrami County Road 706; and</p>
          <p>(4) the existing west access road to the Moose River dike, which is included in meeting the required all-terrain vehicle trail mileage specified in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall sign each road and trail designated under this subdivision indicating the motorized uses allowed.</p>
          <p>(d) During the regular firearms deer season, on all wildlife management area lands within the area described in paragraph (e), a person licensed to take deer may operate an all-terrain vehicle:</p>
          <p>(1) before legal shooting hours;</p>
          <p>(2) after legal shooting hours; and</p>
          <p>(3) from 11:00 a.m. to 2:00 p.m.</p>
          <p>(e) Paragraph (d) applies from where State Highway No. 1 intersects the west boundary of the Red Lake Indian Reservation, then west to State Highway No. 219, then north on State Highway No. 219 to State Highway No. 89, then north on State Highway No. 89 to County Highway No. 6, then east on County Highway No. 6 to County Highway No. 54 and County Highway No. 1 (Beltrami/Marshall county line) then north along the Beltrami/Marshall county line to Roseau county line, then east on Beltrami/Roseau county line to Dick's Parkway, then south on Dick's Parkway to County Road No. 704, Beltrami County, then south to County State-Aid Highway No. 44 to Fourtown, then south on State Highway No. 89 to the north boundary of the Red Lake Indian Reservation, then west and south following the boundary of the Red Lake Indian Reservation to where it intersects State Highway No. 1.</p>
          <p>(f) For the purposes of this subdivision, "corridors of disturbance" means rights-of-way such as ditches, ditch banks, transmission lines, pipelines, permanent roads, winter roads, and recreational trails. The existence of a corridor of disturbance eligible for corridor designation may be demonstrated by physical evidence, document recorded in the office of the county recorder or other public official, aerial survey, or other evidence similar to the above. Cross-country motorized use of land shall not cause that land to be considered a corridor of disturbance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="history" id="stat.97A.133.history
			">
          <h2>History: </h2>
          <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 485 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/353/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 353 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.38.0">2004 c 221 s 38</a></p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.133.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>Agder Wildlife Management Area, Marshall County.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="history" id="stat.97A.133.history
			">
          <h2>History: </h2>
          <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 485 s 16</a>,21,27</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.133.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>Aitkin Wildlife Management Area, Aitkin County.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="history" id="stat.97A.133.history
			">
          <h2>History: </h2>
          <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 485 s 16</a>,21,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/206/#laws.0.11.0">2001 c 206 s 11</a></p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.133.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>Bear Creek Wildlife Management Area, Roseau County.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="history" id="stat.97A.133.history
			">
          <h2>History: </h2>
          <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.16.0">2000 c 485 s 16</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/353/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 353 s 4</a></p>

      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.134">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.134 ADOPT-A-WMA PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.134.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota adopt-a-WMA (wildlife management area) program is established. The commissioner shall coordinate the program through the regional offices of the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.134.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of the program is to encourage sporting, outdoor, business, and civic groups or individuals to assist, on a volunteer basis, in improving and maintaining wildlife management areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.134.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Agreements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall enter into informal agreements with sporting, outdoor, business, and civic groups or individuals for volunteer services to maintain and make improvements to real property on state wildlife management areas in accordance with plans devised by the commissioner after consultation with the groups or individuals.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may erect appropriate signs to recognize and express appreciation to groups and individuals providing volunteer services under the adopt-a-WMA program.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may provide assistance to enhance the comfort and safety of volunteers and to facilitate the implementation and administration of the adopt-a-WMA program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.134.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/98/#laws.0.3.0">2011 c 98 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.50.0">2011 c 107 s 50</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.135">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.135 ACQUIRING WILDLIFE LANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.135.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Public hunting and wildlife areas.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner or the commissioner of administration shall acquire and improve land for public hunting, game refuges, and food and cover planting. The land may be acquired by a gift, lease, easement, purchase, or condemnation. At least two-thirds of the total area acquired in a county must be open to public hunting. The commissioner may designate, by written order published in the State Register, land acquired under this subdivision as a wildlife management area for the purposes of the outdoor recreation system. Designations of wildlife management areas are exempt from the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of administration may transfer money to the commissioner for acquiring wildlife lands to qualify for Pittman-Robertson funds. The transferred money is reappropriated to the commissioner for the wildlife land acquisition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.135.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disposal of unsuitable hunting areas.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall sell or exchange land acquired for public hunting that is unnecessary or unsuitable. The land may not be sold for less than its purchase price. The land may be exchanged for land of equal value that adds to existing public hunting areas. The sales and exchanges must be approved by the Executive Council. This subdivision does not apply to land in a wildlife management area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.135.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Disposal of land in wildlife management areas.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may sell or exchange land in a wildlife management area authorized by designation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.07#stat.86A.07.3">86A.07, subdivision 3</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.133">97A.133</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a> if the commissioner vacates the designation before the sale or exchange in accordance with this subdivision. The designation may be vacated only if the commissioner finds, after a public hearing, that the disposal of the land is in the public interest.</p>
          <p>(b) A sale under this subdivision is subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.09">94.09</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.16">94.16</a>. An exchange under this subdivision is subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.341">94.341</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.347">94.347</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) Revenue received from a sale authorized under paragraph (a) is appropriated to the commissioner for acquisition of replacement wildlife management lands.</p>
          <p>(d) Land acquired by the commissioner under this subdivision must meet the criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.8">86A.05, subdivision 8</a>, and as soon as possible after the acquisition must be designated as a wildlife management area under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.07#stat.86A.07.3">86A.07, subdivision 3</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.133">97A.133</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) In acquiring land under this subdivision, the commissioner must give priority to land within the same geographic region of the state as the land conveyed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.135.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cooperative farming agreements.</span></h2>
          <p>On any public hunting, game refuge, wildlife management area, aquatic management area, or scientific and natural area lands, the commissioner may enter into written cooperative farming agreements on a sharecrop basis, without competitive bidding, for the purpose of wildlife and plant management. Cooperative farming agreements may also be used to allow pasturing of livestock. The agreements may provide for the bartering of a share of any crop, produced from these lands, for services or products that will enhance or benefit the management of state lands for plant and animal species. Cooperative farming agreements pursuant to this section shall not be considered leases for tax purposes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/272.01#stat.272.01.2">272.01, subdivision 2</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.19">273.19</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.135.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.26.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.15.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/605/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 605 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/285/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 285 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.14.0">1994 c 561 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/622/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 622 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/485/#laws.0.17.0">2000 c 485 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.39.0">2004 c 221 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.98.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/121/#laws.0.28.0">2013 c 121 s 28</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.137">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.137 HUNTING, FISHING, AND TRESPASSING IN WILDLIFE MANAGEMENT AREAS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.137.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hunting and fishing.</span></h2>
          <p>Wildlife management areas are open to hunting and fishing unless closed by rule of the commissioner or by posting under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.137.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Closing designated areas.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may, by posting in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.001#stat.97B.001.4">97B.001, subdivision 4</a>, designate areas within wildlife management areas that are closed to entry for the purpose of providing areas where disturbance of wildlife can be minimized. A person may not enter an area posted under this subdivision except as authorized by rule or a permit issued by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.137.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Use of motorized vehicles by people with disabilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may provide an accommodation by issuing a special permit, without a fee, authorizing a person with a disability to use an other power-driven mobility device, as defined under Code of Federal Regulations, title 28, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/35.104">35.104</a>, or a motor boat in wildlife management areas. To qualify for a permit under this subdivision, the person must provide credible assurance to the commissioner that the device or motor boat is used because of a disability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.137.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exemption from certain local ordinances.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Wildlife management areas that are established according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.8">86A.05, subdivision 8</a>; designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.133">97A.133</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a>; and 160 contiguous acres or larger are exempt from local ordinances that limit the taking of game and fish or vegetation management in the unit as authorized by state law.</p>
          <p>(b) Wildlife management areas that are established according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.8">86A.05, subdivision 8</a>; designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.133">97A.133</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a>; and at least 40 contiguous acres and less than 160 contiguous acres are exempt from local ordinances that:</p>
          <p>(1) restrict trapping;</p>
          <p>(2) restrict the discharge of archery equipment;</p>
          <p>(3) restrict the discharge of shotguns with shot sizes of F or .22 inch diameter or smaller shot;</p>
          <p>(4) restrict noise;</p>
          <p>(5) require dogs on a leash; or</p>
          <p>(6) would in any manner restrict the management of vegetation in the unit as authorized by state law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.137.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Portable stands.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Prior to the Saturday on or nearest September 16, a portable stand may be left overnight in a wildlife management area by a person with a valid bear license who is hunting within 100 yards of a bear bait site that is legally tagged and registered as prescribed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.425">97B.425</a>. Any person leaving a portable stand overnight under this subdivision must affix a tag with: (1) the person's name and address; (2) the licensee's driver's license number; or (3) the "MDNR#" license identification number issued to the licensee. The tag must be affixed to the stand in a manner that it can be read from the ground.</p>
          <p>(b) From November 1 through December 31, a portable stand may be left overnight by a person possessing a license to take deer in a wildlife management area located in whole or in part north and west of a line described as follows:</p>
          <p>State Trunk Highway 1 from the west boundary of the state to State Trunk Highway 89; then north along State Trunk Highway 89 to Fourtown; then north on County State-Aid Highway 44, Beltrami County, to County Road 704, Beltrami County; then north on County Road 704 to Dick's Parkway State Forest Road; then north on Dick's Parkway to County State-Aid Highway 5, Roseau County; then north on County State-Aid Highway 5 to Warroad; then north on State Trunk Highway 11 to State Trunk Highway 313; then north on State Trunk Highway 313 to the north boundary of the state.</p>
          <p class="bl">A person leaving a portable stand overnight under this paragraph must affix a tag with: (1) the person's name and address; (2) the licensee's driver's license number; or (3) the "MDNR#" license identification number issued to the licensee. The tag must be affixed to the stand so that it can be read from the ground and must be made of a material sufficient to withstand weather conditions. A person leaving a portable stand overnight in a wildlife management area under this paragraph may not leave more than two portable stands in any one wildlife management area. Unoccupied portable stands left overnight under this paragraph may be used by any member of the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.137.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Crossing state lands.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may grant a permit to cross state lands within wildlife management areas for temporary right-of-way access to federal, county-managed, or privately owned lands for resource management purposes. A permit for crossing state lands within wildlife management areas is revocable at any time subject to conditions identified in the permit.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may grant a permit to a private landowner or leaseholder to cross state lands within wildlife management areas by motorized vehicle for temporary right-of-way access to a permit applicant's land, when it is the only reasonable access and is consistent with the maintenance and management of wildlife lands.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.137.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.18.0">1991 c 259 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.15.0">1993 c 231 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/265/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 265 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.15.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.28.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.17.0">2014 c 290 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.71.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 71</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.43.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 43</a>,44</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.141">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.141 PUBLIC-WATER ACCESS SITES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.141.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition; generally.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall acquire access sites adjacent to public waters and easements and rights-of-way necessary to connect the access sites with public highways. The land may be acquired by gift, lease, or purchase, or by condemnation with approval of the Executive Council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.141.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.40.0">2008 c 357 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.141.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Maintenance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall maintain the sites, easements, and rights-of-way acquired under this section. The commissioner may make an agreement for the maintenance of the site easements and rights-of-way with a county board if the connecting public highway is a county state-aid highway or county highway, or the town board if the connecting highway is a town road. The county board and town board may spend money from its road and bridge funds for maintenance under the agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.141.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cooperation with metropolitan governmental units.</span></h2>
          <p>Local units of government owning lands adjacent to public waters within the seven-county metropolitan area shall cooperate with the commissioner to use those lands for public access purposes when identified by the commissioner under subdivision 1. If cooperation does not occur, the commissioner may use condemnation authority under this section to acquire an interest in the local government lands for public access purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.141.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Hunting generally prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not hunt on water-access sites unless allowed by rule of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.141.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.27.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.26.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 259 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.25.0">2008 c 357 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.145">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.145 WETLANDS FOR WILDLIFE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.145.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition; generally.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner or the commissioner of administration may acquire wetlands and bordering areas, including marshes, ponds, small lakes, and stream bottoms for water conservation relating to wildlife development. The lands that are acquired may be developed for wildlife, recreation, and public hunting. The wetlands may be acquired by gift, lease, purchase, or exchange of state lands.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may also acquire land owned by the state and tax-forfeited land that is suitable for wildlife development. The wetlands may not be acquired unless public access by right-of-way or easement from a public road is also acquired or available. In acquiring wetlands under this section the commissioner shall assign highest priority to type 3 and 4 wetlands, as defined in United States Fish and Wildlife Service Circular No. 39 (1971 edition), that are public waters. Lands purchased or leased under this section may not be used to produce crops unless needed for wildlife. The commissioner may designate, by written order published in the State Register, land acquired under this section as a wildlife management area for purposes of the outdoor recreation system. Designations of wildlife management areas are exempt from the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.145.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acquisition procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Lands purchased or leased under this section must be acquired in accordance with this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must notify the county board and the town officers where the land is located and furnish them a description of the land to be acquired. The county board must approve or disapprove the proposed acquisition within 90 days after being notified. The commissioner may extend the time up to 30 days. The soil and water conservation district supervisors shall counsel the county board on drainage and flood control and the best utilization and capability of the land.</p>
          <p>(c) If the county board approves the acquisition within the prescribed time, the commissioner may acquire the land.</p>
          <p>(d) If the county board disapproves the acquisition, it must state valid reasons. The commissioner may not purchase or lease the land if the county board disapproves the acquisition and states its reasons within the prescribed time period. The landowner or the commissioner may appeal the disapproval to the district court having jurisdiction where the land is located.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner or the owner of the land may submit the proposed acquisition to the Land Exchange Board if:</p>
          <p>(1) the county board does not give reason for disapproval, or does not approve or disapprove the acquisition within the prescribed time period; or</p>
          <p>(2) the court finds that the disapproval is arbitrary and capricious, or that the reasons stated for disapproval are invalid.</p>
          <p>(f) The Land Exchange Board must conduct a hearing and make a decision on the acquisition within 60 days after receiving the proposal.
