The Land Exchange Board must give notice of the hearing to the county board, the commissioner, the landowner, and other interested parties. The Land Exchange Board must consider the interests of the county, the state, and the landowner in determining whether the acquisition is in the public interest. If a majority of the Land Exchange Board members approves the acquisition, the commissioner may acquire the land. If a majority disapproves, the commissioner may not purchase or lease the land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.145.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Management.</span></h2>
          <p>If a drainage outlet is petitioned and drainage proceedings are conducted under the Drainage Code, chapter 103E, the commissioner should not interfere with or unnecessarily delay the proceedings.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.145.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.28.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.3.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.40.0">2004 c 221 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.151">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.151 LEECH LAKE INDIAN RESERVATION AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.151.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of this section is to give recognition and effect to the rights of the Leech Lake Band of Chippewa Indians that are preserved by federal treaty relating to hunting, fishing, and trapping, and to the gathering of wild rice on the Leech Lake Indian Reservation. These rights have been recognized and given effect by the decision of the United States District Court in the following entitled actions: <i>Leech Lake Band of Chippewa Indians, et al. v. Robert L. Herbst</i>, No. 3-69 Civ. 65; and <i>United States of America v. State of Minnesota</i>, No. 3-70 Civ. 228. The state of Minnesota desires to settle all outstanding issues and claims relating to the above rights.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.151.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(a) "Band" means the Leech Lake Band of Chippewa Indians.</p>
          <p>(b) "Committee" means the Reservation Business Committee of the Leech Lake Band of Chippewa Indians.</p>
          <p>(c) "Reservation" means the Leech Lake Indian Reservation described in the settlement agreement.</p>
          <p>(d) "Settlement agreement" means the document entitled "Agreement and Settlement" on file and of record in the United States District Court for the District of Minnesota, Third Division, in the following entitled actions: <i>Leech Lake Band of Chippewa Indians, et al. v. Robert L. Herbst</i>, No. 3-69 Civ. 65; and <i>United States of America v. State of Minnesota</i>, No. 3-70 Civ. 228.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.151.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Ratification of settlement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of any other law to the contrary, the state of Minnesota by this section ratifies and affirms the agreement set forth in the settlement agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.151.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding the provisions of any other law to the contrary, the commissioner, on behalf of the state of Minnesota, shall take all actions, by rule or otherwise, necessary to carry out the duties and obligations of the state of Minnesota arising from the agreement entered into by the parties to the settlement agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) These actions include but are not limited to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the implementation of the exemption of members of the band and other members of the Minnesota Chippewa tribe from state laws relating to hunting, fishing, trapping, the taking of minnows and other bait, and the gathering of wild rice within the reservation, together with exemption from related possession and transportation laws, to the extent necessary to effectuate the terms of the settlement agreement;</p>
          <p>(2) the establishment of a system of special licenses and related license fees for persons who are not members of the Minnesota Chippewa tribe for the privilege of hunting, fishing, trapping, or taking minnows and other bait within the reservation. All money collected by the commissioner for special licenses shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the Leech Lake Band and White Earth Band special license account, which is hereby created. All money in the state treasury credited to the Leech Lake Band and White Earth Band special license account, less any deductions for administrative costs authorized by the terms of the settlement agreement, is appropriated to the commissioner who shall remit the money to the committee pursuant to the terms of the settlement agreement;</p>
          <p>(3) to the extent necessary to effectuate the terms of the settlement agreement, the promulgation of rules for the harvesting of wild rice within the reservation by non-Indians;</p>
          <p>(4) to the extent necessary to effectuate the terms of the settlement agreement, the establishment of policies and procedures for the enforcement by conservation officers of the Conservation Code adopted by the band; and</p>
          <p>(5) the arbitration of disputes arising under the terms of the settlement agreement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.151.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.29.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.155">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.155 AMENDMENTS TO LEECH LAKE INDIAN RESERVATION AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.155.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Payment in lieu of special licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may enter into an agreement with authorized representatives of the Leech Lake Band of Chippewa Indians to amend the settlement agreement adopted by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a> by providing that in lieu of the system of special licenses and license fees for persons who are not members of the Minnesota Chippewa tribe for the privilege of hunting, fishing, trapping, or taking minnows and other bait within the reservation, five percent of the proceeds from all licenses sold in the state for hunting, fishing, trapping, and taking minnows and other bait shall be credited to the special license account established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a>. The funds shall be remitted to the Leech Lake Band in the manner and subject to the terms and conditions that may be mutually agreed upon.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.155.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Payment in lieu of migratory-waterfowl stamp fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may enter into an agreement with the Reservation Business Committee of the Leech Lake Indian Reservation to amend the settlement agreement adopted in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a> by providing that in lieu of collecting an additional fee in connection with the state migratory-waterfowl stamp for the privilege of hunting waterfowl on the Leech Lake Indian Reservation five percent of the proceeds from the sale of state migratory-waterfowl stamps shall be credited to the special license account established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a>. The funds shall be remitted to the Leech Lake Reservation Business Committee in the manner and subject to the terms and conditions provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.155.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Aquaculture allowed.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall not interpret commercial fishing in the settlement agreement in a manner that restricts aquaculture by the Leech Lake Band, or Leech Lake Band members, that is conducted consistent with state policies, laws, and regulations relating to aquaculture.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.155.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.30.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/502/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 502 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.157">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.157 1854 TREATY AREA AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.157.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of this section is to effectuate resolution of issues in dispute between the state of Minnesota and the Grand Portage, Bois Forte, and Fond du Lac Bands of Chippewa Indians that relate to hunting, fishing, trapping, and gathering in the Ceded Area described in the September 30, 1854, treaty between the Lake Superior Chippewa and the government of the United States. This treaty was ratified by the United States Senate on January 10, 1855, and was recognized and affirmed in a subsequent treaty between the Bois Forte Band of Chippewa Indians and the government of the United States dated April 7, 1866, and ratified by the United States Senate on April 26, 1866. The enforcement of certain rights claimed by Lake Superior Chippewas under these treaties has been sought in a civil action brought in the United States District Court for the District of Minnesota, Fourth Division, that is entitled <i>Grand Portage Band of Chippewas, et al. v. State of Minnesota, et al.</i>, Civ. No. 4-85-1090. The state of Minnesota desires to settle all outstanding matters relating to the above dispute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.157.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Settlement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The parties to the above named civil action have negotiated a settlement of the dispute and have filed an executed copy of their "Memorandum of Agreement" with the court on February 16, 1988.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.157.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Ratification of settlement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The state of Minnesota, by the enactment of this section, ratifies and affirms the Memorandum of Agreement, provided the agreement is amended by July 1, 1988, by the addition of the following language: "Any party in the Memorandum of Agreement may cancel this agreement upon one year's written notice to the other parties."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.157.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources, on behalf of the state of Minnesota, shall take all actions, by rule or otherwise, necessary to carry out the duties and obligations of the state of Minnesota arising from the Memorandum of Agreement. Powers and duties provided by this subdivision apply to payment of money under the Memorandum of Agreement only to the extent and to the amount specifically appropriated by the legislature to carry out the terms of the Memorandum of Agreement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.157.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/525/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 525 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.161">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.161 AGREEMENT WITH WHITE EARTH INDIANS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may enter into an agreement with authorized representatives of the White Earth Band of Chippewa Indians on substantially the same terms as the agreement adopted by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a> and amended under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.155">97A.155</a>; except that the agreement shall provide that 2-1/2 percent of the proceeds from all licenses sold in the state for hunting, fishing, trapping, and taking of minnows and other bait shall be credited to the special license account established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151">97A.151</a>. The funds shall be remitted to the White Earth Band in the manner and subject to the terms and conditions that may be mutually agreed upon. An agreement negotiated under this section shall be for a term of at least four years following the date of its execution.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.161.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.31.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 31</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.165">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.165 SOURCE OF PAYMENTS FOR INDIAN AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <p>Money to make payments to the Leech Lake Band, the 1854 Treaty Area agreement, and White Earth Band special license account under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.151#stat.97A.151.4">97A.151, subdivision 4</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.155#stat.97A.155.2">97A.155, subdivision 2</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.157#stat.97A.157.4">97A.157, subdivision 4</a>, is annually appropriated for that purpose from the general fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.165.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.32.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.86.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/561/#laws.0.15.0">1994 c 561 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ENFORCEMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.201">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.201 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.201.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Enforcement by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall execute and enforce the laws relating to wild animals. The commissioner may delegate execution and enforcement of the wild animal laws to the director and enforcement officers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.201.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duty of peace officers.</span></h2>
          <p>All peace officers must enforce the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.201.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prosecuting authority.</span></h2>
          <p>County attorneys are the primary prosecuting authority for violations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.205">97A.205</a>, paragraph (a), clause (5). Prosecution includes associated civil forfeiture actions provided by law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.201.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.33.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.16.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.72.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 72</a>,73</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.205">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.205 ENFORCEMENT OFFICER; POWERS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) An enforcement officer is authorized to:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) execute and serve court issued warrants and processes relating to wild animals, wild rice, public waters, water pollution, conservation, and use of water, in the same manner as a sheriff;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) enter any land to carry out the duties and functions of the division;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) make investigations of violations of the game and fish laws;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) take an affidavit, if it aids an investigation;</p>
        <p class="in">(5) arrest, without a warrant, a person who is detected in the actual violation of the game and fish laws, a provision of chapters 84, 84A, 84D, 85, 86A, 88 to 97C, 103E, 103F, 103G, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.001">86B.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.815">86B.815</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.51">89.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.64">89.64</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.66#stat.609.66.1">609.66, subdivision 1</a>, clauses (1), (2), (5), and (7); and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.68">609.68</a>; and</p>
        <p class="in">(6) take an arrested person before a court in the county where the offense was committed and make a complaint.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Nothing in this section grants an enforcement officer any greater powers than other licensed peace officers.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.205.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.34.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/109/#laws.0.12.0">1987 c 109 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.1.6.0">1987 c 384 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.3.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/558/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 558 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.19.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.3.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.2.4.0">2005 c 10 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.83.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 83</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.211">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.211 ARREST PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.211.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice to appear in court.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person must be given notice to appear in court for a misdemeanor violation of the game and fish laws; chapter 84, 84D, 103E, or 103G; sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.201">103F.201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.221">103F.221</a>; or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.601">103F.601</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.68">609.68</a> if:</p>
          <p>(1) the person is arrested and is released from custody prior to appearing before a court; or</p>
          <p>(2) the person is subject to a lawful arrest and is not arrested because it reasonably appears to the enforcement officer that arrest is unnecessary to prevent further criminal conduct and that there is a substantial likelihood that the person will respond to a notice.</p>
          <p>(b) The enforcement officer shall prepare a written or electronic notice to appear in court as provided by Rules of Criminal Procedure and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.99">169.99</a>. The notice must be in the form and has the effect of a summons and complaint. The notice must contain the name and address of the person charged and the offense. The notice must contain the time and the place to appear in court or must direct the defendant to contact the court or violations bureau to schedule an appearance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.211.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Release after arrest.</span></h2>
          <p>A person arrested for a misdemeanor violation of the game and fish laws; chapter 84, 84D, 103E, or 103G; sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.201">103F.201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.221">103F.221</a>; or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.601">103F.601</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.68">609.68</a> may obtain release by promising to appear in court. The officer shall deliver a notice to the person arrested. The officer must then release the person from custody.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.211.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Court appearance.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before the court appearance date, the enforcement officer must deliver the summons and complaint to the court. If the person summoned fails to appear in court on the day specified, the court shall issue a warrant for the person's arrest.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.211.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.35.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.17.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.24.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 24</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.4.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.12.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 12</a>,13</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.215">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.215 INSPECTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.215.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Storing wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When an enforcement officer has probable cause to believe that wild animals possessed or stored in violation of the game and fish laws are present, the enforcement officer may enter and inspect any commercial cold storage warehouse, hotel, restaurant, ice house, locker plant, butcher shop, and other building used to store dressed meat, game, or fish, to determine whether wild animals are kept and stored in compliance with the game and fish laws.</p>
          <p>(b) When an enforcement officer has probable cause to believe that wild animals taken or possessed in violation of the game and fish laws are present, the officer may:</p>
          <p>(1) enter and inspect any place or vehicle; and</p>
          <p>(2) open and inspect any package or container.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.215.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Records.</span></h2>
          <p>An enforcement officer may inspect the relevant records of any person that the officer has probable cause to believe has violated the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.215.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Licensed activity.</span></h2>
          <p>An enforcement officer may, at reasonable times:</p>
          <p>(1) enter and inspect the premises of an activity requiring a license under the game and fish laws; and</p>
          <p>(2) stop and inspect a motor vehicle requiring a license under the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.215.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Electronic devices.</span></h2>
          <p>During an inspection under subdivision 3, if a person uses an electronic device to display a document to a conservation officer or peace officer:</p>
          <p>(1) the officer is immune from liability for any damage to the device, unless the officer does not exercise due care in handling the device; and</p>
          <p>(2) it does not constitute consent for the officer to access other contents on the device.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.215.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.36.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/169/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 169 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.21.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/92/#laws.0.1.0">2024 c 92 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivision 4, as added by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 21, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.221">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.221 SEIZURE AND CONFISCATION OF PROPERTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.221.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Property subject to seizure and confiscation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An enforcement officer may seize:</p>
          <p>(1) wild animals, wild rice, and other aquatic vegetation taken, bought, sold, transported, or possessed in violation of the game and fish laws or chapter 84 or 84D; and</p>
          <p>(2) firearms, bows and arrows, nets, boats, lines, poles, fishing rods and tackle, lights, lanterns, snares, traps, spears, dark houses, fish houses, and wild rice harvesting equipment that are used with the owner's knowledge to unlawfully take or transport wild animals, wild rice, or other aquatic vegetation and that have a value under $1,000 are subject to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) An item described in paragraph (a), clause (2), that has a value of $1,000 or more is subject to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.225">97A.225</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) An enforcement officer must seize nets and equipment unlawfully possessed within ten miles of Lake of the Woods or Rainy Lake.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.221.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Seizing commingled shipments.</span></h2>
          <p>A whole shipment or parcel is contraband if two or more wild animals are shipped or possessed in the same container, vehicle, or room, or in any way commingled, and any of the animals are contraband. Seizure of any part of a shipment includes the entire shipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.221.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Procedure for confiscating seized property.</span></h2>
          <p>The enforcement officer must hold the seized property. The property held may be confiscated when:</p>
          <p>(1) the person from whom the property was seized is convicted, the conviction is not under appeal, and the time period for appeal of the conviction has expired; or</p>
          <p>(2) the property seized is contraband consisting of a wild animal, wild rice, or other aquatic vegetation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.221.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disposal of confiscated property.</span></h2>
          <p>Confiscated property may be disposed of or retained for use by the commissioner, or sold at the highest price obtainable as prescribed by the commissioner. Upon acquittal or dismissal of the charged violation for which the property was seized:</p>
          <p>(1) all property, other than contraband consisting of a wild animal, wild rice, or other aquatic vegetation, must be returned to the person from whom the property was seized; and</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner shall reimburse the person for any seized or confiscated property that is sold, lost, or damaged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.221.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>The appropriate agency and prosecuting authority shall report on forfeitures of firearms, bows, and motor vehicles occurring under this section as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.5315">609.5315</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.221.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.37.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.19.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.20.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.6.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.22.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/391/#laws.0.2.0">2010 c 391 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.223">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.223 SEIZURE AND ADMINISTRATIVE FORFEITURE OF CERTAIN FIREARMS AND ABANDONED PROPERTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.223.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Property subject to seizure and forfeiture.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An enforcement officer must seize:</p>
          <p>(1) firearms possessed in violation of state or federal law or court order; and</p>
          <p>(2) property described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.221#stat.97A.221.1">97A.221, subdivision 1</a>, where no owner can be determined.</p>
          <p>(b) Property seized under this section is subject to administrative forfeiture.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.223.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice of seizure and intent to forfeit.</span></h2>
          <p>When property is seized under subdivision 1, the enforcement officer shall serve any known owner and person possessing the property with a notice of the seizure and intent to forfeit the property. The notice must be in writing, describing the property seized, the date of seizure, and notice of the right to appeal the seizure and forfeiture as described in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.223.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appeal; final order.</span></h2>
          <p>Seizure and administrative forfeiture of property under this section may be appealed under the procedures in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072#stat.116.072.6">116.072, subdivision 6</a>, if the owner or other person from whom the property was seized requests a hearing by notifying the commissioner in writing within 45 days after seizure of the property. For purposes of this section, the terms "commissioner" and "agency" as used in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a> mean the commissioner of natural resources. If a hearing is not requested within 45 days of seizure, the forfeiture becomes a final order and not subject to further review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.223.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Other remedies.</span></h2>
          <p>The authority to forfeit firearms and other property under this section is in addition to other remedies available under state and federal law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.223.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Disposal of forfeited property.</span></h2>
          <p>Forfeited property under this section may be disposed of as contraband according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.221#stat.97A.221.4">97A.221, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.223.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>The appropriate agency and prosecuting authority shall report on forfeitures of firearms, bows, and motor vehicles occurring under this section as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.5315">609.5315</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.223.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/466/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 466 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/391/#laws.0.3.0">2010 c 391 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.225">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.225 SEIZURE AND CONFISCATION OF MOTOR VEHICLES AND BOATS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seizure.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An enforcement officer must seize all motor vehicles used to:</p>
          <p>(1) shine wild animals in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.081#stat.97B.081.1">97B.081, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) transport big game animals illegally taken or fur-bearing animals illegally purchased; or</p>
          <p>(3) transport minnows in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.501">97C.501</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.515">97C.515</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.525">97C.525</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) An enforcement officer must seize all boats and motors used in netting fish on Lake of the Woods, Rainy Lake, Lake Superior, Namakan Lake, and Sand Point Lake in violation of licensing or operating requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.30">97A.475, subdivision 30</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.825">97C.825</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.831">97C.831</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.835">97C.835</a>, or a rule of the commissioner relating to these provisions.</p>
          <p>(c) An enforcement officer may seize all boats and motors with their trailers that are used to take, possess, or transport wild animals when the restitution value of the wild animals exceeds $500.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Procedure for confiscating seized property.</span></h2>
          <p>The enforcement officer must hold the seized property, subject to the order of the court having jurisdiction where the offense was committed. The property held is confiscated when:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner complies with this section;</p>
          <p>(2) the person from whom it was seized is convicted of the offense; and</p>
          <p>(3) the conviction is not under appeal and the time period for appeal of the conviction has expired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Complaint against property.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall file with the court a separate complaint against the property held. The complaint must identify the property, describe its use in the violation, and specify the time and place of the violation. A copy of the complaint must be served upon the defendant or the owner of the property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Releasing property after posting bond.</span></h2>
          <p>At any time after seizure of the property specified in this section, the property must be returned to the owner or person having the legal right to possession upon execution of a valid bond to the state with a corporate surety. The bond must be approved by a judge of the court of jurisdiction, conditioned to abide by an order and judgment of the court and to pay the full value of the property at the time of seizure. The bond must be for $100 or for a greater amount not more than twice the value of the property seized.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Court order.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the person arrested is acquitted, the court shall dismiss the complaint against the property and:</p>
          <p>(1) order it returned to the person legally entitled to it; and</p>
          <p>(2) order the commissioner to reimburse the person for any seized or confiscated property that is sold, lost, or damaged.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon conviction of the person, the court shall issue an order directed to any person that may have any right, title, or interest in, or lien upon, the seized property. The order must describe the property and state that it was seized and that a complaint against it has been filed. The order shall require a person claiming right, title, or interest in, or lien upon, the property to file with the court administrator an answer to the complaint, stating the claim, within ten days after the service of the order. The order shall contain a notice that if the person fails to file an answer within the time limit, the property may be ordered sold by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The court order must be served upon any person known or believed to have any right, title, interest, or lien in the same manner as provided for service of a summons in a civil action, and upon unknown persons by publication, in the same manner as provided for publication of a summons in a civil action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Court-ordered sale after no answer.</span></h2>
          <p>If an answer is not filed within the time provided in subdivision 5, the court administrator shall notify the court and the court shall order the commissioner to sell the property. The net proceeds of the sale shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the game and fish fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Hearing after answer.</span></h2>
          <p>If an answer is filed within the time provided in subdivision 5, the court shall schedule a hearing within ten to 30 days after the time expired for filing the answer. The court, without a jury, shall determine whether any of the property was used in a violation specified in the complaint and whether the owner had knowledge or reason to believe that the property was being used, or intended to be used, in the violation. The court shall order the commissioner to sell the property that was unlawfully used with knowledge of the owner and to return to the owner property that was not unlawfully used with the knowledge of the owner. If the property is to be sold, the court shall determine the priority of liens against the property and whether the lienholders had knowledge that the property was being used or was intended to be used. Lienholders that had knowledge of the property's use in the violation are not to be paid. The court order must state the priority of the liens to be paid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Proceeds of sale.</span></h2>
          <p>The proceeds from the sale after payment of the costs of seizing, towing, keeping, and selling the property and satisfying valid liens against the property must be distributed as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) 70 percent of the money or proceeds shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the game and fish fund; and</p>
          <p>(2) 30 percent of the money or proceeds is considered a cost of forfeiting the property and must be forwarded to the prosecuting authority that handled the forfeiture for deposit as a supplement to its operating fund or similar fund for prosecutorial purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Security interests canceled.</span></h2>
          <p>A sale under this section cancels all liens on and security interests in the property sold.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.225.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>The appropriate agency and prosecuting authority shall report on forfeitures occurring under this section as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.5315">609.5315</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.225.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.38.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/270/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 270 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.24.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 24</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/391/#laws.0.4.0">2010 c 391 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.74.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 74</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.231">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.231 SEARCH WARRANTS.</h1>
        <p>Upon complaint establishing that the complainant has probable cause to believe that a wild animal taken, bought, sold, transported, or possessed in violation of the game and fish laws, or contraband, is concealed or illegally kept in a place, a judge, authorized to issue warrants in criminal cases, may issue a search warrant. The judge may direct that the place be entered, broken open, and examined. Property seized under the warrant shall be safely kept under the direction of the court so long as necessary for the purpose of being used as evidence in a trial and subsequently disposed of as otherwise provided.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.231.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.39.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 39</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.235">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.235 JURISDICTION OVER BOUNDARY WATERS.</h1>
        <p>Courts in counties having jurisdiction adjacent to boundary waters and enforcement officers have jurisdiction over the entire boundary waters. The courts and enforcement officers of North Dakota, South Dakota, Iowa, Wisconsin, and Michigan have concurrent jurisdiction over boundary waters.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.235.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.40.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 40</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.241">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.241 RECIPROCITY WITH OTHER STATES IN APPOINTING OFFICERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.241.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Officers of other jurisdictions as special conservation officers.</span></h2>
          <p>With approval of the proper authority of another state or the United States, the commissioner may appoint any salaried and bonded officer of that jurisdiction authorized to enforce its wild animal laws a special conservation officer of this state. A special conservation officer is subject to the supervision and control of and serves at the pleasure of the commissioner, but may not be compensated by this state. A special conservation officer has powers of and is subject to the liabilities of conservation officers of this state, except as otherwise directed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.241.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Officers of this state as officers of other jurisdictions.</span></h2>
          <p>An enforcement officer or peace officer of this state may enforce wild animal laws of another state, or the United States, under conditions prescribed by the commissioner. The officer may serve under the laws of another jurisdiction to the extent they are compatible with the duties of an officer of this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.241.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reciprocal effect.</span></h2>
          <p>This section is effective with respect to another state or the United States to the extent that there is a similar provision in effect in that jurisdiction with respect to this state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.241.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.41.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.245">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.245 REWARDS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may pay rewards for information leading to the conviction of a person that has violated a provision of laws relating to wild animals or threatened or endangered species of wildlife. A reward may not exceed $500, except a reward for information relating to big game or threatened or endangered species of wildlife, may be up to $1,000 and a reward for information relating to wolves may be up to $2,500. The rewards may only be paid from funds donated to the commissioner for these purposes and may not be paid to salaried conservation officers or peace officers.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.245.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.42.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/401/#laws.0.34.0">1998 c 401 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/463/#laws.0.22.0">2000 c 463 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.90.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 90</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.251">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.251 OBSTRUCTING OFFICERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.251.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Unlawful conduct.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not:</p>
          <p>(1) intentionally hinder, resist, or obstruct an enforcement officer, agent, or employee of the division in the performance of official duties;</p>
          <p>(2) refuse to submit to inspection of equipment used to take wild animals while in the field, licenses, or wild animals; or</p>
          <p>(3) refuse to allow inspection of a motor vehicle, boat, or other conveyance used while taking or transporting wild animals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.251.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Civil actions.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to criminal prosecution, the state may bring a civil action to recover damages resulting from and enjoin the continuance of a violation of this section. The civil actions may be brought by the attorney general on the request of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.251.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.43.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.26.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.255">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.255 PROSECUTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.255.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Statute of limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>A prosecution under the game and fish laws may not be brought more than three years after commission of the offense.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.255.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In a prosecution that alleges animals have been taken, bought, sold, transported, or possessed in violation of the game and fish laws, the burden of establishing that the animals were domesticated, reared in a private preserve, raised in a private fish hatchery or aquatic farm, taken for scientific purposes, lawfully taken, or received as a gift, is on the defendant.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may by rule prescribe the documentation or other evidence sufficient to demonstrate lawful possession of:</p>
          <p>(1) a wild animal received as a gift; and</p>
          <p>(2) a wild animal taken on an Indian reservation or in another state, province, or country.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.255.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.54.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 54</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.255.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Separate offenses; prosecution of aggregated offenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as allowed in paragraph (b), each wild animal unlawfully taken, bought, sold, transported, or possessed is a separate offense. If acquitted, a person may not be prosecuted for a similar offense involving another animal in the same incident.</p>
          <p>(b) In any prosecution that involves two or more offenses committed by the same person within six months in two or more counties, the accused may be prosecuted in any county in which one of the offenses was committed for all of the offenses in aggregate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.255.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Joint and several liability.</span></h2>
          <p>When two or more people intentionally aid, advise, counsel, conspire with, or act in concert with each other to unlawfully take, transport, or possess wild animals when the restitution value of the wild animals exceeds $500, each person is jointly and severally liable for the wild animals for purposes of:</p>
          <p>(1) license seizure, invalidation, and revocation under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.420">97A.420</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.421">97A.421</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) equipment and property seizure under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.221">97A.221</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) boat, motor, and trailer seizure under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.225">97A.225</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) restitution under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.341">97A.341</a>.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.255.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.44.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.20.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 231 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/270/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 270 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.14.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.22.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 5 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 22, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <h1 class="fgh">PENALTIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.301">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.301 GENERAL PENALTY PROVISIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.301.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Misdemeanor.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless a different penalty is prescribed, a person is guilty of a misdemeanor if that person:</p>
          <p>(1) violates the game and fish laws;</p>
          <p>(2) aids or assists in committing the violation;</p>
          <p>(3) knowingly shares in the proceeds of the violation;</p>
          <p>(4) fails to perform a duty or comply with a requirement of the game and fish laws;</p>
          <p>(5) knowingly makes a false statement related to an affidavit regarding a violation or requirement of the game and fish laws; or</p>
          <p>(6) violates or attempts to violate a rule under the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.301.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Gross misdemeanor.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless a different penalty is prescribed, a person convicted of violating a provision of the game and fish laws that is defined as a gross misdemeanor is subject to a fine of not less than $100 nor more than $3,000 and imprisonment in the county jail for not less than 90 days or more than 364 days.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.45.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.75.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.6.16.0">2023 c 52 art 6 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.305">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.305 IMPERSONATING ENFORCEMENT OFFICER.</h1>
        <p>A person that purports to be acting in an official capacity and causes another to be injured or defrauded while falsely impersonating an enforcement officer or other officer acting under authority of the game and fish laws, or falsely claiming to have special authority under those laws, is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.305.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.46.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 46</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.311">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.311 LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.311.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Altering license.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that alters a license in a material manner is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.311.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">False statement.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that knowingly makes a false statement related to an application for a license, a license, or certificate, required by or issued under the game and fish laws, is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.311.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">License agent violations.</span></h2>
          <p>A license agent that knowingly issues a license to an ineligible person or predates a license is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.311.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">License agent suspension.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to other penalties, a license agent that violates a law or rule of the commissioner relating to license sales, handling, or accounting forfeits the right to sell and handle licenses for a period of one year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.311.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Refunds.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner may issue a refund on a license, not including any issuing fees paid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.6">97A.485, subdivision 6</a>, if the request is received within 90 days of the original license purchase and:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the licensee dies before the opening of the licensed season. The original license and a copy of the death certificate must be provided to the commissioner;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the licensee is unable to participate in the licensed activity because the licensee is called to active military duty or military leave is canceled during the entire open season of the licensed activity. The original license and a copy of the military orders or notice of cancellation of leave must be provided to the commissioner;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the licensee purchased two licenses for the same license season in error;</p>
          <p>(4) the licensee was not legally required to purchase the license to participate in the activity; or</p>
          <p>(5) evidence is provided to the commissioner that demonstrates the license was issued incorrectly by the department or license agent.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This subdivision does not apply to lifetime licenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.311.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">License corrections.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may correct a license or license type and refund the difference or charge the difference of the corrected license fee if:</p>
          <p>(1) the licensee provides evidence that the license was issued incorrectly by the department or license agent;</p>
          <p>(2) the request is made within 30 days of the original license purchase;</p>
          <p>(3) the season or license activities for the original license have not yet started at the time of the request; and</p>
          <p>(4) the licensee is entitled to the corrected license.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.311.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.47.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.21.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.28.0">2004 c 255 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.19.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.23.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 290 s 19</a>,20</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.315">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.315 TRESPASS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.315.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Criminal penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), a person that violates a provision of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.001">97B.001</a>, relating to trespass is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) A person is guilty of a gross misdemeanor if the person:</p>
          <p>(1) knowingly disregards signs prohibiting trespass;</p>
          <p>(2) trespasses after personally being notified by the landowner or lessee not to trespass; or</p>
          <p>(3) is convicted of violating this section more than once in a three-year period.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.101#stat.609.101.4">609.101, subdivision 4</a>, clause (2), for a misdemeanor violation, the minimum fine for a person who operates an off-highway motorcycle, off-road vehicle, all-terrain vehicle, or snowmobile in violation of this section must not be less than the amount set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.775">84.775</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.315.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License revocations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a person is convicted of trespassing under subdivision 1 while exercising or attempting to exercise an activity licensed under the game and fish laws or requiring snowmobile registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.82">84.82</a>, the applicable license and registration are null and void.</p>
          <p>(b) A person convicted of a gross misdemeanor under subdivision 1, paragraph (b), may not be issued a license to take game for two years after the conviction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.315.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.48.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.22.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.45.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 45</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.321">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.321 DOGS PURSUING, WOUNDING, OR KILLING BIG GAME.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.321.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Owner responsibility; penalty amount.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The owner of a dog that pursues but does not kill or mortally wound a big game animal is subject to a civil penalty of $100 for each violation. The owner of a dog that kills or mortally wounds a big game animal is subject to a civil penalty of $500 for each violation.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to a person using a dog in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.207">97B.207</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.321.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appeals.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil penalties under this section may be appealed according to procedures in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072#stat.116.072.6">116.072, subdivision 6</a>, if the person requests a hearing by notifying the commissioner in writing within 15 days after receipt of the citation. If a hearing is not requested within the 15-day period, the civil penalty becomes a final order not subject to further review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.321.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil penalties under this section may be enforced according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072, subdivisions 9</a> and 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.321.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Paying penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Penalty amounts shall be remitted to the commissioner within 30 days of issuance of the penalty notice and shall be deposited in the game and fish fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.321.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.49.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.27.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.21.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.24.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.49.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 49</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.325">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.325 PENALTIES FOR UNLAWFULLY BUYING OR SELLING WILD ANIMALS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.325.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Gross misdemeanor for sales of $300 or more.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person that buys or sells protected wild animals in violation of the game and fish laws where the sales total $300 or more is guilty of a gross misdemeanor. The person is subject to the penalty in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.301#stat.97A.301.2">97A.301, subdivision 2</a>, except that the fine may not be less than $3,000 or more than $10,000.</p>
          <p>(b) Licenses possessed by a person convicted under this subdivision are null and void and the person may not take wild animals for three years after the conviction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.325.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Deer; bear; moose; elk; caribou.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 1, a person that violates a provision of the game and fish laws relating to buying or selling deer, bear, moose, elk, or caribou is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.325.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Small game and game fish.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 1, a person that buys or sells small game or game fish in violation of the game and fish laws where the sales total $50 or more is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.325.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Fur-bearing animals.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 1, a person that buys fur-bearing animals in violation of the game and fish laws is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.325.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.50.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.23.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.27.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.331">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.331 PENALTIES RELATED TO HUNTING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hunting while under the influence of alcohol or a controlled substance.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that violates the provision of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.065">97B.065</a> relating to hunting while under the influence of alcohol or a controlled substance is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Shining.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that violates section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.081">97B.081</a>, subdivision 1, relating to the use of an artificial light to locate wild animals is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Transporting illegal big game.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that knowingly transports big game taken in violation of the game and fish laws is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Taking and possessing big game out of season.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that takes or illegally possesses big game during the closed season is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Hunting big game while under revocation.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.421#stat.97A.421.7">97A.421, subdivision 7</a>, a person who takes big game during the time the person is prohibited from obtaining a license to take big game under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.421">97A.421</a> is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Moose; elk; caribou.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that unlawfully takes, transports, or possesses moose, elk, or caribou in violation of the game and fish laws is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Pine marten; otter; fisher; wolverine.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that takes, transports, or possesses pine marten, otter, fisher, or wolverine in violation of the game and fish laws is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.331.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.91.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 91</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.331.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.51.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.24.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/463/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 463 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.17.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.25.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.335">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.335 PENALTIES RELATED TO FISHING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.335.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Taking fish with illegal devices or substances.</span></h2>
          <p>A person that takes fish with devices, chemicals or substances in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.325">97C.325</a>, is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.335.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Illegally taking or possessing muskellunge.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who takes or possesses a muskellunge in violation of the game and fish laws is guilty of a misdemeanor and subject to a fine of up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.335.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.52.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 52</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.338">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.338 GROSS OVERLIMITS OF WILD ANIMALS; PENALTY.</h1>
        <p>(a) A person who takes, possesses, or transports wild animals over the legal limit, in closed season, or without a valid license, when the restitution value of the wild animals is over $1,000 is guilty of a gross overlimit violation. Except as provided in paragraph (b), a violation under this paragraph is a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        <p>(b) If a wild animal involved in a gross overlimit violation is listed as a threatened or endangered wild animal, the penalty in paragraph (a) does not apply unless more than one animal is taken, possessed, or transported in violation of the game and fish laws.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.338.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/270/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 270 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.76.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 76</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.341">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.341 RESTITUTION FOR WILD ANIMALS ILLEGALLY TAKEN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.341.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Liability for restitution.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who kills, injures, or possesses a wild animal in violation of the game and fish laws or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/343.21">343.21</a> is liable to the state for the value of the wild animal as provided in this section. Species afforded protection include members of the following groups as defined by statute or rule: game fish, native rough fish, game birds, big game, small game, fur-bearing animals, minnows, and threatened and endangered animal species. Other animal species may be added by rule of the commissioner as determined after public meetings and notification of the chairs of the environment and natural resources committees in the senate and house of representatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.341.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Arrest and charging procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An enforcement officer who arrests a person for killing, injuring, or possessing a wild animal in violation of the game and fish laws or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/343.21">343.21 </a>must describe the number, species, and restitution value of wild animals illegally killed, injured, or possessed on the warrant or the notice to appear in court.</p>
          <p>(b) As part of the charge against a person arrested for killing, injuring, or possessing a wild animal in violation of the game and fish laws or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/343.21">343.21</a>, the prosecuting attorney must include a demand that restitution be made to the state for the value of the wild animal killed, injured, or possessed. The demand for restitution is in addition to the criminal penalties otherwise provided for the violation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.341.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sentencing procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person is convicted of or pleads guilty to killing, injuring, or possessing a wild animal in violation of the game and fish laws or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/343.21">343.21</a>, the court must require the person to pay restitution to the state for replacement of the wild animal as part of the sentence or state in writing why restitution was not imposed. The court may consider the economic circumstances of the person and, in lieu of monetary restitution, order the person to perform conservation work representing the amount of restitution that will aid the propagation of wild animals. If the court does not order a person to pay restitution, the court administrator must send a copy of the court order to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.341.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Amount of restitution.</span></h2>
          <p>The amount of restitution shall be determined by the court by a preponderance of the evidence. In determining the amount of restitution, the court must consider the value of the wild animal under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.345">97A.345</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.341.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Restitution credited to game and fish fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The court administrator shall forward restitution collected under this section to the commissioner of management and budget and the commissioner shall credit all money forwarded to the game and fish fund in the state treasury.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.341.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/298/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 298 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.2.13.0">2024 c 90 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.27.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 27</a>-29</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.345">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.345 RESTITUTION VALUE OF WILD ANIMALS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner may, by rules adopted under chapter 14, prescribe the dollar value to the state of species of wild animals. The value may reflect the value to other persons to legally take the wild animal, the replacement cost, or the intrinsic value to the state of the wild animals. Species of wild animals with similar values may be grouped together.</p>
