        <p>(b) The value of a wild animal under the rules adopted by the commissioner is prima facie evidence of a wild animal's value under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.341">97A.341</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner shall report annually to the legislature the amount of restitution collected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.341">97A.341</a> and the manner in which the funds were expended.</p>
        <p>(d) When a person kills, injures, or possesses a wild animal in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/343.21">343.21</a>, the restitution value prescribed by the commissioner under paragraph (a) is doubled.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.345.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/298/#laws.0.3.0">1989 c 298 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.30.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LICENSES AND PERMITS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.401">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.401 SPECIAL PERMITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue special permits for the activities in this section. A special permit may be issued in the form of a general permit to a governmental subdivision or to the general public to conduct one or more activities under subdivisions 2 to 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Collecting zoological specimens.</span></h2>
          <p>Special permits may be issued without a fee to municipalities, incorporated natural history societies, high schools, colleges, and universities that maintain a zoological collection, to collect specimens of eggs, nests, and wild animals for scientific or exhibition purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Taking, possessing, and transporting wild animals for certain purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), special permits may be issued without a fee to take, possess, and transport wild animals as pets and for scientific, educational, rehabilitative, wildlife disease prevention and control, and exhibition purposes. The commissioner shall prescribe the conditions for taking, possessing, transporting, and disposing of the wild animals.</p>
          <p>(b) A special permit may not be issued to take or possess wild or native deer for exhibition, propagation, or as pets.</p>
          <p>(c) Nonresident professional wildlife rehabilitators with a federal rehabilitation permit may possess and transport wildlife affected by oil spills.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Taking wild animals from game refuges, wildlife management, and other areas.</span></h2>
          <p>Special permits may be issued, with or without a fee, to take a wild animal from game refuges, wildlife management areas, state parks, controlled hunting zones, and other areas of the state that the commissioner may open for the taking of a wild animal during a special season or subject to special restrictions. In addition, an application fee may be charged for a special permit. Local units of government may charge an administrative fee in connection with special hunts under their jurisdiction. Fees to be collected shall be based upon the estimated cost of conducting the special season or administering the special restrictions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Wild animals damaging property.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Special permits may be issued with or without a fee to take protected wild animals or to remove or destroy their dens, nests, eggs, houses, or dams for the purpose of preventing or reducing damage or injury to people, property, agricultural crops, or other interests. The commissioner may prescribe rules for taking Canada geese and their nests and eggs, with or without a permit, consistent with federal regulations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Endangered muskrats.</span></h2>
          <p>Special permits may be issued with or without a fee to take muskrats in danger of freezing out or starving in the winter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Raptors.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prescribe conditions and may issue permits for persons to breed, propagate, and sell raptors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Snakes, lizards, and salamanders.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must prescribe conditions and may issue permits to breed, propagate, and sell snakes, lizards, and salamanders. A snake, lizard, or salamander that is obtained from a permitted breeder or that was possessed before August 1, 2021, may be possessed as a pet.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.401.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Taking wild animals with federal incidental take permit.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must prescribe conditions for and may issue a permit to a person for taking wild animals during activities covered under a federal incidental take permit issued under section 10(a)(1)(B) of the federal Endangered Species Act, including to a landowner for taking wild animals during activities covered by a certificate of inclusion issued by the commissioner under Code of Federal Regulations, title 50, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.25">13.25</a>(e).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.53.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/450/#laws.0.2.0">1986 c 450 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/287/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 287 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.17.0">1993 c 231 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.21.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.24.0">1996 c 410 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.55.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/146/#laws.0.9.0">2005 c 146 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.21.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.24.0">2012 c 272 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.65.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.53.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 53</a>,54; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.46.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 46</a>,47</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.405">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.405 LICENSE REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Protected wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless allowed under the game and fish laws, a person may not take, buy, sell, transport, or possess protected wild animals of this state without a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Personal possession.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A person acting under a license or traveling from an area where a licensed activity was performed must have in personal possession:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the proper paper license;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a driver's license or Minnesota identification card that bears a valid designation of the proper lifetime license, as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.19">171.07, subdivision 19</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the proper paper license identification number or stamp validation, if the license has been sold to the person by electronic means; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) electronic or other evidence satisfactory to the commissioner that the person has the proper paperless license.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If possession of a license or a license identification number is required, a person must exhibit, as requested by a conservation officer or peace officer: (1) the proper paper license; (2) a driver's license or Minnesota identification card that bears a valid designation of the proper lifetime license, as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.19">171.07, subdivision 19</a>; (3) the proper paper license identification number or stamp validation and a valid state driver's license, state identification card, or other form of identification provided by the commissioner; or (4) electronic or other evidence satisfactory to the commissioner that the person has the proper paperless license. A person charged with violating the license possession requirement shall not be convicted if the person produces in court or the office of the arresting officer, the actual license previously issued to that person, which was valid at the time of arrest, or satisfactory proof that at the time of the arrest the person was validly licensed. Upon request of a conservation officer or peace officer, a licensee shall write the licensee's name in the presence of the officer to determine the identity of the licensee.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Except as provided in paragraph (a), clauses (2) and (4), if the actual license has been issued and received, a receipt for license fees, a copy of a license, or evidence showing the issuance of a license, including the license identification number or stamp validation, does not entitle a licensee to exercise the rights or privileges conferred by a license.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A pictorial migratory waterfowl, pheasant, trout and salmon, or walleye stamp shall be provided to the licensee after purchase of a stamp validation only if the licensee pays an additional fee that covers the costs of producing and mailing a pictorial stamp. A pictorial turkey stamp may be purchased for a fee that covers the costs of producing and mailing the pictorial stamp. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the commissioner may, by written order published in the State Register, establish fees for providing the pictorial stamps. The fees must be set in an amount that does not recover significantly more or less than the cost of producing and mailing the stamps. The fees are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a), (b), and (c), a person may use a digital image of a paper license issued to and received by the person to meet the requirement to possess the proper paper license under paragraph (a), clause (1), and may exhibit to a conservation officer or peace officer a digital image of a paper license issued to and received by the person to meet the requirements of paragraph (b). This paragraph expires March 5, 2025.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duplicate licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prescribe rules for issuing duplicate licenses to persons whose licenses are lost or destroyed.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Replacement deer licenses.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner may permit licensed deer hunters to change zone, license, or season options. The commissioner may issue a replacement deer license if the applicant pays any increase in cost between the original and the replacement deer license. A refund of the difference in fees may be issued when a person changes from a regular deer license to a youth deer license.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A replacement deer license may be issued only if the applicant has not harvested a deer under the original deer license or licenses and meets the conditions of paragraph (c).</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A replacement deer license may be issued under the following conditions, or as otherwise prescribed by rule of the commissioner:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) when the season for the deer license being surrendered has not yet opened; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) when the person is changing from a regular deer license to a youth deer license.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.411#stat.97A.411.3">97A.411, subdivision 3</a>, a replacement deer license is valid immediately upon issuance if the deer license being surrendered is valid at that time.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Replacement turkey licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may permit licensed turkey hunters to change permit areas, licenses, or time periods within the fall turkey season, or within the spring turkey season. The commissioner may issue a replacement turkey license if the applicant pays the fee for a replacement license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.44">97A.475, subdivision 44</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A replacement turkey license may be issued only if the applicant has not harvested a turkey under the original turkey license and meets the requirements of paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(c) A turkey replacement license may be issued under the following conditions, or as otherwise prescribed by rule of the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) when the permit area or time period for the turkey license being surrendered has not yet opened; and</p>
          <p>(2) licenses are available for the replacement turkey license permit area or time period for (i) areas that are not lottery areas, (ii) lottery areas that have remaining licenses, or (iii) the applicant is a youth hunter age 17 or younger.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Resident licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To obtain a resident license, an individual 21 years of age or older must be a resident and:</p>
          <p>(1) possess a current Minnesota driver's license or a valid application receipt for a driver's license that is at least 60 days past the issuance date;</p>
          <p>(2) possess a current identification card issued by the commissioner of public safety or a valid application receipt for an identification card that is at least 60 days past the issuance date;</p>
          <p>(3) present evidence showing proof of residency in cases when clause (1) or (2) would violate the Religious Freedom Restoration Act of 1993, Public Law 103-141; or</p>
          <p>(4) possess a Tribal identification card as provided in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "Tribal identification card" means an unexpired identification card as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.072">171.072</a>, paragraphs (b) and (c). The Tribal identification card:</p>
          <p>(1) must contain the enrolled Tribal member's Minnesota residence address; and</p>
          <p>(2) may be used to obtain a resident license under paragraph (a) only if the Tribal member does not have a current driver's license or state identification card in any state.</p>
          <p>(c) A person must not have applied for, purchased, or accepted a resident hunting, fishing, or trapping license issued by another state or foreign country within 60 days before applying for a resident license under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.405.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Application deadline.</span></h2>
          <p>When an application deadline is specified, including an application deadline for determining the fee based on age for a lifetime license, an application must be received no later than 4:30 p.m. on the day of the deadline or, if mailed, an application must be postmarked on or before the deadline date.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.405.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.54.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.25.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.81.0">1997 c 216 s 81</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.31.0">2000 c 495 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.106.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/146/#laws.0.10.0">2005 c 146 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.28.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.84.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.22.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.20.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.18.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.26.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.29.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 29</a>,30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.21.0">2014 c 290 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.3.37.0">2016 c 189 art 3 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.50.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.48.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 48</a>,49; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.23.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 23</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/92/#laws.0.2.0">2024 c 92 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 2 by Laws 2023, chapter 60, article 4, section 48, is effective March 1, 2026. Laws 2023, chapter 60, article 4, section 48, the effective date.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendments to subdivisions 2, 3, 4, and 4a by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, sections 23 to 26, are effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.408">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.408 ANATOMICAL GIFT OPTION; EDUCATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Applications through the Department of Natural Resources' online sales system for a resident license to hunt or fish that is required under this chapter or chapter 97B must allow the applicant to indicate a desire to make an anatomical gift according to paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(b) The online sales system application must contain statements sufficient to comply with the requirements of the Darlene Luther Revised Uniform Anatomical Gift Act, chapter 525A, so that execution of the application makes the anatomical gift effective for those indicating a desire to make an anatomical gift. The department website must provide a link to a federally designated organ procurement organization website that contains information describing Minnesota laws regarding anatomical gifts, the need for and benefits of anatomical gifts, and the legal implications of making an anatomical gift, including the law governing revocation of anatomical gifts.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner must provide education by distributing information about organ and tissue donation and how to register as a donor. The commissioner must distribute the information through Department of Natural Resources print and digital communications targeting those who hunt and fish. The commissioner must prepare the information in conjunction with a Minnesota organ procurement organization.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner is not required to keep the physical record of the donor's application after issuing the license in order for the anatomical gift to be valid. When an individual indicates consent on an online sales system transaction to share private data for the record of registered donors, the commissioner must provide the private name and address of the individual to the organ procurement organization designated by the federal government. The names and addresses are classified as private data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0874">84.0874 </a>and remain private once provided to the organ procurement organization.</p>
        <p>(e) Chapter 525A applies to anatomical gifts made on an application for a license under this section.</p>
        <p>(f) The department must be reimbursed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.075#stat.171.075.1">171.075, subdivision 1</a>, for the reasonable costs of administering an anatomical gift program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.408.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/131/#laws.0.2.0">2016 c 131 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.411">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.411 VALIDITY OF LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License period.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in paragraphs (b), (d), (e), and (f), a license is valid during the lawful time within the license year that the licensed activity may be performed. Except as provided in paragraphs (c) and (f), a license year begins on the first day of March and ends on the last day of February.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A short-term license issued under section 97A.475 that is limited by the number of days or hours under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475">97A.475</a>, is valid for the full license period even if this period extends into the next license year, provided that the license period selected by the licensee begins at the time of issuance.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The license year for resident fish house, resident dark house, and nonresident fish house begins on March 1 and ends on April 30 of the following year.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A lifetime license issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.474">97A.474</a> is valid during the lawful time within the license year that the licensed activity may be performed for the lifetime of the licensee.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A three-year fish house or dark house license is valid during the license year that it is purchased and the two succeeding license years.</p>
          <p>(f) A three-year individual angling license is valid during the license year in which it is purchased and the two succeeding license years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.82.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 82</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.411.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Deer license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraphs (b) and (c), a license to take deer by archery, firearms, or muzzleloader issued after the opening of the related archery, firearms, or muzzleloader deer season, respectively, is not valid unless it was issued prior to legal shooting hours on the day of its first use.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may issue a license to take additional deer under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.301#stat.97B.301.4">97B.301, subdivision 4</a>, that is not valid unless it was issued prior to legal shooting hours on the day the license is first used.</p>
          <p>(c) Paragraph (a) does not apply to deer licenses for discharged military personnel under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.465#stat.97A.465.4">97A.465, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.411.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Validity of license when age or residency status changes.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to take wild animals that was lawfully obtained continues to be valid for the balance of the license period if the licensee's age, residency, or student qualification status changes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.55.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/462/#laws.0.13.0">1992 c 462 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.25.0">1996 c 410 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.19.0">1997 c 226 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/252/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 252 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/341/#laws.0.3.0">2000 c 341 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.107.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 107</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.85.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.24.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.2.3.0">2012 c 277 art 2 s 3</a>,4,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.15.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.415">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.415 LICENSE RESTRICTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">One license per person.</span></h2>
          <p>Only one trapping and big-game license of each kind may be issued to a person in a license year unless authorized by commissioner's rule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not lend, transfer, borrow, or solicit a license or permit, license identification number, application for a license or permit, coupon, tag, or seal, or use a license, permit, license identification number, coupon, tag, or seal not issued to the person unless otherwise expressly authorized. A person may transfer a license, as prescribed by the commissioner, for use by a person with a severe disability or critical illness who is participating in a hunting or fishing program sponsored by a nonprofit organization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.415.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Nonresidents.</span></h2>
          <p>Nonresidents may not obtain a license for an activity unless the activity is expressly authorized for nonresidents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.56.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.18.0">1993 c 231 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.82.0">1997 c 216 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.27.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.418">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.418 PERMIT RULES.</h1>
        <p>Wherever the game and fish laws specifically provide for the issuance of a permit by the commissioner, the commissioner may do the following in accordance with criteria and procedures established in rules adopted by the commissioner:</p>
        <p>(1) issue a permit with reasonable conditions; and</p>
        <p>(2) deny, modify, suspend, or revoke a permit for cause, including violation of the game and fish laws or rules adopted thereunder.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.418.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.19.0">1993 c 231 s 19</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.420">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.420 SEIZURE OR INVALIDATION OF LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.420.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seizure or invalidation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An enforcement officer shall immediately seize or invalidate the license of a person who unlawfully takes, transports, or possesses wild animals when the restitution value of the wild animals exceeds $500. Except as provided in subdivisions 2, 4, and 5, the person may not use or obtain any license to take the same type of wild animals involved, including a duplicate license, until an action is taken under subdivision 6. If the license seized or invalidated under this paragraph was for a big game animal, the license seizure or invalidation applies to all licenses to take big game issued to the individual. If the license seized or invalidated under this paragraph was for small game animals, the license seizure or invalidation applies to all licenses to take small game issued to the individual.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the license seizure or invalidation under paragraph (a), if the restitution value of the wild animals unlawfully taken, possessed, or transported is $1,000 or more, all other game and fish licenses held by the person shall be immediately seized or invalidated. Except as provided in subdivision 2, 4, or 5, the person may not obtain any game or fish license or permit, including a duplicate license, until an action is taken under subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not take wild animals covered by a license seized or invalidated under this subdivision until an action is taken under subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner must make a means of seizing or invalidating and releasing a paperless license under this section available to enforcement officers.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.420.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administrative review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) At any time after the seizure or invalidation of a license under subdivision 1 and before revocation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.421">97A.421</a>, a person may request in writing a review of the seizure or invalidation under this section. Upon receiving the request for review, the commissioner shall review the seizure or invalidation, the evidence upon which it was based, and other material information brought to the attention of the commissioner, and determine whether sufficient cause exists to sustain the seizure or invalidation.</p>
          <p>(b) Within 15 days after receiving the request for administrative review, the commissioner shall issue a written report of the review and shall order that the seizure or invalidation be either sustained or rescinded.</p>
          <p>(c) The review provided in this subdivision is not subject to the contested case provisions of the Administrative Procedure Act under chapter 14. The availability of administrative review does not preclude judicial review under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.420.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Judicial review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 30 days following the seizure or invalidation of a license under subdivision 1, a person may petition the court for review. The petition must be filed with the district court administrator in the county where the incident occurred, together with proof of service of a copy on the commissioner and the county attorney. A responsive pleading is not required of the commissioner of natural resources and court fees may not be charged for the appearance of the representative of the commissioner in the matter.</p>
          <p>(b) The petition must be captioned in the name of the person making the petition as petitioner and the commissioner as respondent. The petition must state specifically the grounds upon which the petitioner seeks rescission of the license seizure or invalidation.</p>
          <p>(c) The filing of the petition does not stay the license seizure or invalidation. The judicial review shall be conducted according to the Rules of Civil Procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.420.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A hearing under subdivision 3 must be before a district court judge in the county where the incident occurred giving rise to the license seizure or invalidation. The hearing must be to the court and may be conducted at the same time as hearings upon pretrial motions in a related criminal prosecution. The commissioner must be represented by the county attorney.</p>
          <p>(b) The hearing must be held at the earliest practicable date and in any event no later than 60 days following the filing of the petition for review.</p>
          <p>(c) The scope of the hearing must be limited to the issue of whether there is probable cause to believe that the person had unlawfully taken, possessed, or transported wild animals with a restitution value over $500.</p>
          <p>(d) The court shall order that the license seizure or invalidation be either sustained or rescinded. Within 14 days following the hearing, the court shall forward a copy of the order to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(e) Any party aggrieved by the decision of the reviewing court may appeal the decision as provided in the Rules of Civil Appellate Procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.420.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Temporary release of commercial licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>At any time during the period that a game or fish license is seized or invalidated under subdivision 1, a person possessing a commercial license issued under the game and fish laws may make a written request to the commissioner to temporarily release the commercial license. If the commissioner determines that the public welfare will not be injured, the commissioner may temporarily reinstate the commercial license upon payment of a temporary reinstatement fee of $1,000 cash or bond in favor of the state for each commercial license to be released. An additional fee is not required for vehicles licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.475#stat.97A.475.26">97A.475, subdivision 26</a>, clause (2) or (4). If the license is returned under subdivision 6, paragraph (a), the temporary reinstatement fee shall be returned to the licensee. If the license is revoked under subdivision 6, paragraph (b), the temporary reinstatement fee shall be deposited in the game and fish fund and is not refundable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.420.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Action upon dismissal or conviction.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Upon acquittal, dismissal, or determination not to charge a person for a violation, the license seizure or invalidation under subdivision 1 is immediately rescinded and any license seized or invalidated in connection with the incident must be returned to the licensee or reinstated.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon conviction of a violation when the restitution value of the wild animals exceeds $500, revocation of licenses and license privileges must be imposed as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.421#stat.97A.421.2a">97A.421, subdivision 2a</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.420.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/270/#laws.0.5.0">2002 c 270 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/215/#laws.0.8.0">2004 c 215 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/121/#laws.0.29.0">2013 c 121 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.77.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 77</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.50.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.27.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 1 by Laws 2023, chapter 60, article 4, section 50, is effective March 1, 2026. Laws 2023, chapter 60, article 4, section 50, the effective date.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendments to this section by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 27, are effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.421">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.421 VALIDITY AND ISSUANCE OF LICENSES AFTER CONVICTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The annual license of a person convicted of a violation of the game and fish laws relating to the license or wild animals covered by the license is void when:</p>
          <p>(1) a second conviction occurs within three years under a license to trap fur-bearing animals, take small game, or to take fish by angling or spearing;</p>
          <p>(2) a second conviction occurs within three years under a minnow dealer's license;</p>
          <p>(3) a second conviction occurs within three years for violations of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.425">97A.425 </a>that do not involve falsifications or intentional omissions of information required to be recorded, or attempts to conceal unlawful acts within the records;</p>
          <p>(4) two or more misdemeanor convictions occur within a three-year period under a private fish hatchery license;</p>
          <p>(5) the conviction occurs under a license not described in clause (1), (2), or (4) or is for a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.425">97A.425</a> not described in clause (3); or</p>
          <p>(6) the conviction is related to assisting a person in the illegal taking, transportation, or possession of wild animals, when acting as a hunting or angling guide.</p>
          <p>(b) Except for big-game licenses and as otherwise provided in this section, for one year after the conviction the person may not obtain the kind of license or take wild animals under a lifetime license, issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.474">97A.474</a>, relating to the game and fish law violation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Issuance after conviction; buying and selling wild animals.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not obtain a license to take any wild animal or take wild animals under a lifetime license, issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.474">97A.474</a>, for a period of three years after being convicted of buying or selling game fish, native rough fish, big game, or small game, and the total amount of the sale is $300 or more.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Issuance after conviction; gross overlimits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not obtain a license to take a wild animal and is prohibited from taking wild animals for ten years after the date of conviction of a violation when the restitution value of the wild animals is $2,000 or more.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may not obtain a license to take a wild animal and is prohibited from taking wild animals for five years after the date of conviction of:</p>
          <p>(1) a violation when the restitution value of the wild animals is $1,000 or more, but less than $2,000; or</p>
          <p>(2) a violation when the restitution value of the wild animals exceeds $500 and the violation occurs within ten years of one or more previous license revocations under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not obtain a license to take the type of wild animals involved in a violation when the restitution value of the wild animals exceeds $500 and is prohibited from taking the type of wild animals involved in the violation for three years after the date of conviction of a violation.</p>
          <p>(d) The time period of multiple revocations under paragraph (a) or (b), clause (2), is consecutive and no wild animals of any kind may be taken during the entire revocation period.</p>
          <p>(e) If a wild animal involved in the conviction is listed as a threatened or endangered wild animal, the revocations under this subdivision do not apply unless more than one animal is taken, possessed, or transported in violation of the game and fish laws.</p>
          <p>(f) The court may not stay or reduce the imposition of license revocation provisions under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Issuance after conviction; big game.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not use a big-game license purchased before conviction, obtain a big-game license, or take big game under a lifetime license, issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a>, for three years after the person is convicted of:</p>
          <p>(1) a gross misdemeanor violation under the game and fish laws relating to big game;</p>
          <p>(2) doing an act without a required big-game license; or</p>
          <p>(3) the second violation within three years under the game and fish laws relating to big game.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may not obtain any deer license or take deer under a lifetime license issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a> for one year after the person is convicted of hunting deer with the aid or use of bait under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.328">97B.328</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The revocation period under paragraphs (a) and (b) doubles if the conviction is for a deer that is a trophy deer scoring higher than 170 using the scoring method established for wildlife restitution values adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.345">97A.345</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Issuance after conviction; firearm suppressor.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who is convicted of a violation under paragraph (b) and possessed a firearm with a suppressor may not obtain a hunting license or hunt wild animals for five years from the date of conviction.</p>
          <p>(b) The revocation under this subdivision applies to convictions of:</p>
          <p>(1) trespass as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.315#stat.97A.315.1">97A.315, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(2) hunting game in closed season;</p>
          <p>(3) hunting game more than one-half hour before legal shooting hours or more than one-half hour after legal shooting hours; or</p>
          <p>(4) using artificial lights to spot, locate, or take wild animals while in possession of a firearm.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Issuance after conviction; night vision or thermal imaging equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who is convicted of a violation under paragraph (b) and who possessed night vision or thermal imaging equipment during the violation may not obtain a hunting license or hunt wild animals for five years from the date of conviction.</p>
          <p>(b) The revocation under this subdivision applies to convictions for:</p>
          <p>(1) trespassing;</p>
          <p>(2) hunting game in closed season;</p>
          <p>(3) hunting game in closed hours;</p>
          <p>(4) possessing night vision or thermal imaging equipment while taking wild animals in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.086">97B.086</a>; or</p>
          <p>(5) possessing unlawful firearms in deer zones in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.041">97B.041</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Issuance after conviction; intoxication or narcotics.</span></h2>
          <p>A person convicted of a violation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.065">97B.065</a>, relating to hunting while intoxicated or using narcotics, may not obtain a license to hunt with a firearm or by archery or hunt with a firearm or by archery under a lifetime license, issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.473">97A.473</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.474">97A.474</a>, for five years after conviction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Suspension for failure to appear in court or pay fine or surcharge.</span></h2>
          <p>When a court reports to the commissioner that a person: (1) has failed to appear in court in response to a notice to appear or fails to comply with other orders of the court regarding the appearance or proceedings for a violation of the game and fish laws; or (2) has been convicted of violating a provision of the game and fish laws, has been sentenced to the payment of a fine or had a surcharge levied against them, and refused or failed to comply with that sentence or to pay the fine or surcharge, the commissioner shall suspend the game and fish license and permit privileges of the person until notified by the court that the person has appeared in court under clause (1) or that any fine or surcharge due the court has been paid under clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commissioner reinstatement.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that the public welfare will not be injured, the commissioner may reinstate licenses voided under subdivision 1 and issue licenses to persons ineligible under subdivision 2 or 2a. The commissioner's authority applies only to licenses to:</p>
          <p>(1) maintain and operate fur or game farms, aquatic farms, or private fish hatcheries;</p>
          <p>(2) take fish by commercial netting;</p>
          <p>(3) buy fish from commercial netting licensees; and</p>
          <p>(4) sell or export turtles or live minnows.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Applicability to moose or elk license applications.</span></h2>
          <p>In this section, the term "license" includes an application for a license to take either moose or elk.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.421.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Taking wild animals while privileges are suspended.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person who takes a protected wild animal during the time the person is prohibited from obtaining a license to take that animal under this section is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.421.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.57.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.27.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/373/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 373 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/589/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 589 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.21.0">1997 c 226 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/341/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 341 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.26.0">2001 c 185 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/270/#laws.0.6.0">2002 c 270 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/351/#laws.0.6.0">2002 c 351 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/215/#laws.0.9.0">2004 c 215 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.25.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.19.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.25.0">2012 c 272 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.31.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/65/#laws.3.3.0">2015 c 65 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.78.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.55.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 55</a>,56; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.51.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.2.14.0">2024 c 90 art 2 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.425">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.425 RECORD AND REPORTING REQUIREMENTS FOR DEALERS, TANNERS, AND TAXIDERMISTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.425.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License and record requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person must have the required license under the game and fish laws to buy or sell wild animals, to tan animal hides or dress raw furs, or to mount specimens of wild animals and must keep complete records of all transactions and activities covered by the license and submit reports to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) A person is not required to be licensed to tan animal hides or dress raw furs or to mount specimens of wild animals if the person is not compensated for the service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.425.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Records.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The records must show:</p>
          <p>(1) the names and addresses of persons from whom wild animals were obtained and to whom they were transferred;</p>
          <p>(2) the dates of receipt, shipment, and sale of wild animals;</p>
          <p>(3) detailed descriptions of the number and type of wild animals purchased, sold, and shipped;</p>
          <p>(4) serial numbers of seals, tags, or permits required to be attached to the wild animals; and</p>
          <p>(5) trapping license numbers for protected fur-bearing animals, unless the trapper is exempt from the license requirement, which must be noted.</p>
          <p>(b) A licensed fur dealer, buying for one employer at the employer's place of business, is not required to keep separate records if the employer notifies the commissioner in writing that the employer will account for the fur dealer.</p>
          <p>(c) The records required under this section must be available for inspection by the commissioner, the director, or their agents at all reasonable times. The records must be preserved and available for two years after the expiration of a license that required them.</p>
          <p>(d) Records required of persons licensed to buy or sell wild animals, or to tan or dress raw furs, must be kept in a book supplied by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.425.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for persons licensed to mount specimens of wild animals, an annual report covering the preceding license year must be submitted to the commissioner by March 15. The commissioner may require other reports for statistical purposes. The reports must be on forms supplied or approved by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.425.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Waste disposal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Licensed taxidermists must dispose of all cervid carcasses or cervid parts not returned to the patron, all biosolids resulting from cleaning cervid skulls, and all carrion beetles and beetle waste used to clean cervid skulls. All disposals must be to a disposal facility or transfer station that is permitted to accept it, and proof of the disposal must be retained for inspection.</p>
          <p>(b) The following cervid parts are exempt from the disposal requirement:</p>
          <p>(1) cervid hides from which all excess tissue has been removed;</p>
          <p>(2) if free of brain and muscle tissues, whole or portions of skulls, antlers, or teeth; and</p>
          <p>(3) finished taxidermy mounts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.425.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules, not inconsistent with subdivisions 1 to 3a, governing record keeping, reporting, and marking of specimens by taxidermists.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.425.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.58.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.28.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/589/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 589 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.27.0">2001 c 185 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.28.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.31.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 31</a>,32</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.431">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.431 MOOSE LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.431.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Number of licenses; party size.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall include in a rule setting the dates for a moose season:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of licenses to be issued; and</p>
          <p>(2) the size of a moose hunting party, not to exceed six persons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.431.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Persons eligible for a moose license shall be determined under this section and commissioner's rule. A person is eligible for a moose license only if the person:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is a resident; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) has not been issued a moose license after January 1, 1991.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.431.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application for license.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a moose license must be on a form provided by the commissioner and accompanied by a $4 nonrefundable application fee per person. A person may not make more than one application for each season. If a person makes more than one application, the person is ineligible for a license for that season after determination by the commissioner, without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.431.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Separate selection; eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may conduct a separate selection for up to 20 percent of the moose licenses to be issued for an area. Only owners of, and tenants living on, at least 160 acres of agricultural or grazing land in the area, and their family members, are eligible for the separate selection under this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must conduct a separate selection for 20 percent of the moose licenses to be issued each year. Only individuals who have applied at least ten times for a moose license and who have never received a license are eligible for this separate selection.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may by rule establish criteria for:</p>
          <p>(1) determining eligible family members under paragraph (a); and</p>
          <p>(2) verifying that an individual has made at least ten unsuccessful applications for the purposes of paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(d) A person who is unsuccessful in a separate selection under this subdivision must be included in the selection for the remaining licenses.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.431.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.59.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/373/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 373 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.28.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 231 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/473/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 473 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.32.0">2000 c 495 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.21.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.32.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.433">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.433 ELK LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.433.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Number of licenses; party size.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall include in a rule setting the dates for an elk season:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of licenses to be issued; and</p>
          <p>(2) the size of an elk-hunting party, not to exceed two persons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.433.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Persons eligible for an elk license shall be determined under this section and commissioner's rule. A person is eligible for an elk license only if the person:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is a resident; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) has never been issued an elk license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.433.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application for license.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for an elk license must be on a form provided by the commissioner and accompanied by a $4 nonrefundable application fee per person. A person may not make more than one application for each season. If a person makes more than one application, the person is ineligible for a license for that season after determination by the commissioner, without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.433.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Discretionary separate selection; eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may conduct a separate selection for up to 20 percent of the elk licenses to be issued for an area. Only owners of, and tenants living on, at least 160 acres of agricultural or grazing land in the area, and their family members, are eligible for the separate selection. Persons that are unsuccessful in a separate selection must be included in the selection for the remaining licenses. Persons who obtain an elk license in a separate selection may sell their license to any Minnesota resident eligible to hunt big game for no more than the original cost of the license.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may by rule establish criteria for determining eligible family members under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.433.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Mandatory separate selection.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must conduct a separate selection for 20 percent of the elk licenses to be issued each year. Only individuals who have applied at least ten times for an elk license and who have never received a license are eligible for this separate selection. A person who is unsuccessful in a separate selection under this subdivision must be included in the selection for the remaining licenses.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.433.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/373/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 373 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.22.0">1993 c 231 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.22.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.29.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.33.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.51.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 51</a>,52</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.434">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.434 PRAIRIE-CHICKEN LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.434.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Number of licenses to be issued.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner establishes an open season for prairie chickens, the commissioner shall also determine, by rule, the number of licenses to be issued.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.434.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Eligibility for a prairie-chicken license shall be determined by this section and by rule adopted by the commissioner. A person is eligible for a prairie-chicken license only if the person is a resident.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.434.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application for license.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a prairie-chicken license must be made in a manner provided by the commissioner and accompanied by a $4 application fee. The $4 application fee is appropriated as prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (c), to pay for costs associated with conducting the prairie-chicken-license drawing. A person may not make more than one application for each season. If a person makes more than one application, the person is ineligible for a license for that season after determination by the commissioner, without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.434.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Separate selection of eligible licensees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may conduct a separate selection for up to 20 percent of the prairie-chicken licenses to be issued for any area. Only persons who are owners or tenants of at least 40 acres of prairie or grassland in the area, and their family members, are eligible applicants for prairie-chicken licenses for the separate selection. The qualifying prairie or grassland may be noncontiguous. Persons who are unsuccessful in a separate selection must be included in the selection for the remaining licenses. Persons who obtain a license in a separate selection must allow public prairie-chicken hunting on their land during that prairie-chicken season.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may by rule establish criteria for determining eligible family members under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.434.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/351/#laws.0.7.0">2002 c 351 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.29.0">2004 c 255 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.23.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.22.0">2014 c 290 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.435">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.435 TURKEY LICENSES; APPLICATION AND ELIGIBILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.435.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License issuance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall include in rules setting turkey seasons the methods for issuing licenses for those seasons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.435.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons eligible for a turkey license shall be determined by this section and commissioner's rule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.435.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application for license.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a turkey license must be on a form provided by the commissioner and accompanied by a $4 application fee. A person may not make more than one application for each season. If a person makes more than one application the person is ineligible for a license for that season after determination by the commissioner, without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.435.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Separate selection of eligible licensees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may conduct a separate selection for up to 20 percent of the turkey licenses to be issued for any area. Only persons who are owners or tenants of and who live on at least 40 acres of land in the permit area, and their family members who live on the qualifying land, are eligible applicants for turkey licenses for the separate selection. The qualifying land may be noncontiguous. Persons who are unsuccessful in a separate selection must be included in the selection for the remaining licenses. Persons who obtain a license in a separate selection must allow public turkey hunting on their land during that turkey season. A license issued under this subdivision is restricted to the permit area where the qualifying land is located.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may by rule establish criteria for determining eligible family members under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.435.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.435.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.1.60.0">1986 c 386 art 1 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/588/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 588 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.29.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 259 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.24.0">1993 c 231 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/473/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 473 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.33.0">2000 c 495 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/215/#laws.0.10.0">2004 c 215 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/146/#laws.0.12.0">2005 c 146 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.30.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.34.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 34</a>; art 2 s 5,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.16.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.438">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.438 RELEASING WILD TURKEYS; PERMIT REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>A person may not release a wild turkey or wild-turkey hybrid without a permit from the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.97A.438.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.25.0">1993 c 231 s 25</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.97A.441">
        <h1 class="shn">97A.441 LICENSES ISSUED WITHOUT FEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Angling and spearing; disabled residents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person authorized to issue licenses must issue, without a fee, licenses to take fish by angling or spearing to a resident who is:</p>
          <p>(1) blind;</p>
          <p>(2) a recipient of Supplemental Security Income for the aged, blind, and disabled;</p>
          <p>(3) a recipient of Social Security aid to the disabled under United States Code, title 42, section 416, paragraph (i)(l), or section 423(d);</p>
          <p>(4) a recipient of workers' compensation based on a finding of total and permanent disability;</p>
          <p>(5) 65 years of age or older and was qualified under clause (2) or (3) at the age of 64;</p>
          <p>(6) permanently disabled and meets the disability requirements for Supplemental Security Income or Social Security aid to the disabled under United States Code, title 42, section 416, paragraph (i)(l), or section 423(d);</p>
          <p>(7) receiving aid under the federal Railroad Retirement Act of 1974, United States Code, title 45, section 231a(a)(1)(v); or</p>
          <p>(8) a former employee of the United States Postal Service receiving disability pay under United States Code, title 5, section 8337.</p>
          <p>(b) A driver's license or Minnesota identification card bearing the applicable designation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.17">171.07, subdivision 17</a>, serves as satisfactory evidence to obtain a license under this subdivision at all agent locations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Angling; foreign exchange students.</span></h2>
          <p>A license to take fish by angling shall be issued without a fee to a citizen of a foreign country that is attending school in this state as an exchange student.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Angling; residents of state institutions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a license, without a fee, to take fish by angling to a person that is a ward of the commissioner of human services and a resident of a state institution upon application by the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Angling; developmentally disabled residents.</span></h2>
          <p>A person authorized to issue licenses must issue, without a fee, a permanent license to take fish by angling to a resident at least 16 years old that is developmentally disabled upon being furnished satisfactory evidence of the disability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Angling; disabled veterans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person authorized to issue licenses must issue, without a fee, a permanent license to take fish by angling to a resident who is a veteran, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/197.447">197.447</a>, and that has a 100 percent service-connected disability as defined by the United States Veterans Administration upon being furnished satisfactory evidence.</p>
          <p>(b) A driver's license or Minnesota identification card bearing the designation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.15">171.07, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), serves as satisfactory evidence to obtain a license under this subdivision at all agent locations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Taking deer; disabled veterans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person authorized to issue licenses must issue, without a fee, a license to take deer with firearms or by archery to a resident that is a veteran, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/197.447">197.447</a>, and that has a 100 percent service-connected disability as defined by the United States Veterans Administration upon being furnished satisfactory evidence.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner, upon request, must issue a permanent card documenting satisfactory evidence of 100 percent permanently disabled status.</p>
          <p>(c) The following serve as satisfactory evidence to obtain a license under this subdivision at all agent locations:</p>
          <p>(1) a card issued under paragraph (b); or</p>
          <p>(2) a driver's license or Minnesota identification card bearing the designation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.15">171.07, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Taking small game; disabled veterans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person authorized to issue licenses must issue, without a fee, a license to take small game to a resident who is a veteran, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/197.447">197.447</a>, and who has a 100 percent service-connected disability as defined by the United States Veterans Administration upon being furnished satisfactory evidence.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner, upon request, must issue a permanent card documenting satisfactory evidence of 100 percent permanently disabled status.</p>
          <p>(c) The following serve as satisfactory evidence to obtain a license under this subdivision at all agent locations:</p>
          <p>(1) a card issued under paragraph (b); or</p>
          <p>(2) a driver's license or Minnesota identification card bearing the designation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.15">171.07, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.97A.441.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Owners or tenants of agricultural land.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner may issue, without a fee, a license to take an antlerless deer to a resident who is an owner or tenant, or a nonresident who is an owner, of at least 80 acres of agricultural land, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.001">97B.001</a>, in deer permit areas that allow the taking of antlerless deer without a lottery application. A person may receive only one license per year under this subdivision. For properties with co-owners or cotenants, only one co-owner or cotenant may receive a license under this subdivision per year. The license issued under this subdivision is restricted to land leased for agricultural purposes or owned by the holder of the license within the permit area where the qualifying land is located. The holder of the license may transfer the license to the holder's spouse or dependent. Deer taken under this subdivision do not count towards the total bag limit for the permit area.
